GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 32.01 RETURNED CHECKS.
   There shall be a $30 charge made for any check returned for insufficient
funds or for any check returned for insufficient funds or for any other reason.
(Prior Code, § 34.01) (Ord. 187-89, passed 10- -1989; Res. 02-106, passed 12-9-
2002)
§ 32.02 SALES TAX REQUIRED ON EMPLOYEE PURCHASES.
   (A)   An employee purchasing an item at any store on town accounts for
personal use will have to pay sales tax on that item.
   (B)   (1)   All departments must fill out purchase orders for an item(s)
purchased for the town.
      (2)   These purchase orders must have at least two Council members’
signatures before picking up the item(s) unless the item(s) is needed for an
emergency and Council members cannot be reached in this matter.
(Prior Code, § 34.02) (Res. 88-100, passed - -)
§ 32.03 COUNTY OPTION INCOME TAX.
   (A)   The Council agrees to raise the county option income tax to $0.07 to
help the county government with increased costs due to the new juvenile center.
   (B)   This increase became effective as of July 1, 1993.
(Prior Code, § 34.03) (Res. 93-139, passed 3-8-1993)
§ 32.04 PROCUREMENT POLICY.
   (A)   Purpose. To establish procedures for purchasing goods and services in
accordance with I.C. Title 5, Article 22, ensuring transparency, efficiency,
and legal compliance.
   (B)   Scope. This policy applies to all purchases made by the town,
including supplies, services, and public works projects, unless specifically
exempted by statute.
   (C)   Definitions. Terms used herein shall have the meanings defined in I.C.
5-22-2, including the following.
      PURCHASING AGENT. The Clerk-Treasurer or designee authorized to make
purchases.
      SERVICES. Includes professional, technical, and consulting services.
      SUPPLIES. Includes goods, equipment, and materials.
   (D)   Purchasing authority.
      (1)   The Town Council designates the Clerk-Treasurer as the Purchasing
Agent under I.C. 5-22-4.
      (2)   The Purchasing Agent may delegate authority for purchases under
$5,000.
   (E)   Procurement thresholds and methods.
 
Purchase Type       Dollar Threshold  Method Required       Statutory Reference
Competitive bidding > $50,000         Formal sealed bids;   I.C. 5-22-7
                                      public opening
Request for         Any amount        Used for services     I.C. 5-22-9
proposals (RFP)                       requiring evaluation
                                      Informal quotes;
Small purchases     $25,000 - $50,000 documentation         I.C. 5-22-8
                                      required
                                      Includes sole source,
Special methods     Varies            emergency, and the    I.C. 5-22-10
                                      like
 
   (F)   Purchasing preferences. In accordance with I.C. 5-22-15, preference
shall be given to:
      (1)   Indiana businesses;
      (2)   Recycled products;
      (3)   Products manufactured in the United States; and
      (4)   Veterans.
   (G)   Contract requirements. All contracts entered into by the town shall
comply with I.C. 5-22-17 and must include the following.
      (1)   Written agreement. All contracts must be in writing and include:
         (a)   Scope of work or services;
         (b)   Term and renewal provisions;
         (c)   Payment terms and schedule; and
         (d)   Termination clauses and remedies.
      (2)   Council approval. Contracts exceeding $25,000 or involving
liability must be approved by the Town Council and reviewed by legal counsel.
      (3)   Eligibility for public funds.
         (a)   Contractors must certify they are eligible to receive state and
federal funds.
         (b)   Proof of eligibility shall include verification of active
registration in SAM.gov and absence from any state or federal debarment lists.
         (c)   Documentation must be submitted to the Council prior to contract
execution.
      (4)   Build America, Buy America (BABA) compliance.
         (a)   For contracts involving infrastructure projects funded by
federal financial assistance, contractors must comply with the Build America,
Buy America Act (Pub. Law No. 117-58, §§ 70901 through 70927).
         (b)   This includes ensuring that all iron, steel, manufactured
products, and construction materials used in the project are produced in the
United States.
         (c)   Contractors shall provide documentation demonstrating BABA
compliance, including material origin certifications and procurement records,
as required by 2 C.F.R. part 184 and relevant agency guidance.
      (5)   Federal compliance clauses. Contracts involving federal funds shall
include applicable clauses required under 2 C.F.R. part 200 (Uniform Guidance),
including:
         (a)   Equal Employment Opportunity;
         (b)   Davis-Bacon Act (if applicable);
         (c)   Byrd Anti-Lobbying Amendment; and
         (d)   Contract Work Hours and Safety Standards Act.
   (H)   Recordkeeping.
      (1)   All purchasing records shall be retained for at least five years
per I.C. 5-22-18.
      (2)   Records include quotes, bids, contracts, and Council minutes.
   (I)   Exceptions.
      (1)   Emergency purchases under I.C. 5-22-10-4 must be documented and
reported to the Council within five business days.
      (2)   Purchases from other governmental units or cooperative purchasing
agreements are permitted under I.C. 5-22-10-5.
   (J)   Ethics and conflicts of interest.
      (1)   Town officials and employees shall comply with I.C. 5-22-16
regarding conflicts of interest and disclosure requirements.
      (2)   No employee or official shall participate in a procurement decision
in which they have a financial interest.
(Ord. 102-25, passed 8-11-2025)
CUMULATIVE CAPITAL DEVELOPMENT FUND
§ 32.15 FUND RE-ESTABLISHED.
   There is re-established the Town Cumulative Capital Development Fund.
(Prior Code, § 34.30) (Ord. 221-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 221-95, passed 5-8-
1995; Ord. 234-96, passed 5-13-1996)
§ 32.16 LEVY IMPOSED.
   (A)   (1)   An ad valorem property tax levy will be imposed, and the
revenues from the levy will be retained in the Town Cumulative Capital
Development Fund.
      (2)   The maximum rate of levy under division (A)(1) above will not
exceed $0.15 per $100 assessed valuation.
(Prior Code, § 34.31)
   (B)   The current Cumulative Capital Development Fund tax rate passed by the
town is adopted and incorporated as part of this code of ordinances as fully as
if set out at length herein.
(Ord. 221-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 221-95, passed 5-8-1995; Ord. 234-96,
passed 5-13-1996; Ord. 105-15, passed 7-13-2015)
§ 32.17 TERM OF FUND.
   The Town Cumulative Capital Development Fund is re-established for the year
1997 and will continue as long as they are advertised and adopted in the budget
unless the municipality decides to rescind or reduce the fund.
(Prior Code, § 34.32) (Ord. 221-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 221-95, passed 5-8-
1995; Ord. 234-96, passed 5-13-1996)
§ 32.18 USE OF FUND.
   (A)   The funds accumulated in the Town Cumulative Capital Development Fund
will be used for additional street repairs, as stated in I.C. 36-9-16.5;
sidewalk repairs as stated in I.C. 36-9-27-100; or other Cumulative Capital
Improvement Fund projects as stated in I.C. 36-9-16-2 and I.C. 36-9-16-3.
   (B)   Notwithstanding division (A) above, funds accumulated in the Town
Cumulative Capital Development Fund may be spent for purposes other than the
purposes stated in §
32.17 if the purpose is to protect the public health, welfare, or safety, which
is in immediate danger that requires the expenditure of money in the fund.
(Prior Code, § 34.33) (Ord. 221-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 221-95, passed 5-8-
1995; Ord. 234-96, passed 5-13-1996)
§ 32.19 EFFECTIVE UPON TAX COMMISSIONER’S APPROVAL.
   This fund takes effect upon approval of the State Board of Tax
Commissioners.
(Prior Code, § 34.34) (Ord. 221-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 221-95, passed 5-8-
1995; Ord. 234-96, passed 5-13-1996)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 33.001 INTENT AND SCOPE.
   (A)   It shall be the policy of the town to recruit and select qualified
persons for positions in the town’s service. The town is committed to
recruitment practices that appeal to all geographic areas and types of people
within the town.
   (B)   This recruitment and selection shall be conducted in an affirmative
manner to insure open and fair competition, provide equal employment
opportunity, and to prohibit discrimination because of race, religion, sex,
national origin, age, or mental or physical handicap.
(Prior Code, § 35.001) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.002 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   FULL-TIME EMPLOYEE. An employee who fills a regular town position and who
works in the office seven hours per day and 35 hours per week, or in a position
not associated with the office and works eight hours per day and 40 hours per
week, including a 30-minute lunch (clocked out and in) and two 15-minute breaks
(on the clock), and who is employed on a year-round basis.
   PART-TIME EMPLOYEE. An employee who fills a temporary need and who may or
may not be employed on a year-round basis.
   SEASONAL EMPLOYEE. An employee who works only during certain portions of the
year.
(Prior Code, § 35.002) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
RECRUITMENT AND SELECTION
§ 33.015 APPLICATION FORMS.
   Applications for employment will be available from the Clerk-Treasurer at
the Town Hall. Such forms shall require background information including
training, experience, and other pertinent data. All applications must be signed
by the applicant, and the Council may require proof of statements on the form.
Applications shall not elicit any information concerning age, race, religion,
national origin, or any information deemed illegal according to EEO
regulations. Such data may be obtained separately for reporting purposes but
not used to discriminate. All applications shall be kept on file for a period
of six months.
(Prior Code, § 35.010) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.016 REJECTION OF APPLICATIONS.
   Any application which indicates the applicant does not meet the minimum
qualifications established for the position that is being applied for may be
rejected. Applications may also be rejected if the applicant:
   (A)   Has deliberately falsified the application;
   (B)   Has established an unsatisfactory employment record of such nature
that it demonstrates unsuitability for the position applied for; and/or
   (C)   Is found by the Council to be clearly unable to perform the job
applied for.
(Prior Code, § 35.011) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.017 ANNOUNCEMENT OF OPENINGS.
   In order to attract an adequate number of candidates, the Council may issue
job announcements and otherwise publicize vacancies through such media as is
deemed appropriate.
(Prior Code, § 35.012) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.018 EMPLOYMENT PROCESS IN GENERAL.
   Interviews and screenings for openings will be made by the Council and
department head of the appropriate department. All personnel except deputies of
the Clerk-Treasurer shall be hired with review of the Council and approval of
the Council. The Clerk-Treasurer shall notify the Council before any applicant
can be placed on the payroll.
(Prior Code, § 35.013) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.019 DISTRIBUTION OF PERSONNEL POLICY MANUAL.
   At the time of adoption, a copy of these personnel rules and regulations
will be given to all current employees, and an employee meeting will be held
explaining it. Thereafter, all new employees shall receive a copy of this
policy and it will be explained to them by their supervisors. Changes and
additions to the policy shall be presented to employees and added to the
existing policy.
(Prior Code, § 35.014) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT
§ 33.030 PROBATIONARY PERIOD.
   (A)   All new employees except Deputy Marshals and reserves shall undergo a
probationary period of 90 days.
      (1)   Deputy Marshals and reserves shall have a probationary period of
one year.
      (2)   Probationary employees shall be eligible for benefits as permanent
town employees after 90 days with regard to holidays, sick leave, vacation, and
the like.
   (B)   During the probationary period, job training shall be provided for the
employee, and a review of progress is recommended at the end of each month.
These review periods shall be used to assess the employee in relation to the
job or position and allow the employee to better evaluate his or her attitude
toward the town and the job.
   (C)   If a review of progress is not satisfactory during the probationary
period, an employee is subject to dismissal without recourse. At the end of the
probationary period, the department head and the Council shall evaluate the
employee.
(Prior Code, § 35.030) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.031 HOURS OF WORK.
   (A)   The designated work hours for each department will be recommended by
the department head with approval of the Council. All employees should be aware
that conditions arise which make it necessary for them to work outside of usual
hours as needed and directed by their supervisor.
   (B)   Overtime pay will be compensated at the rate of one-and-a-half hours
for every hour worked beyond the normal 40-hour work week.
   (C)   Employees of the Police Department will work a 28-day work cycle with
possible accumulation of 171 hours worked before overtime pay is granted.
Department heads and the Council shall follow guidelines set forth by the Fair
Labor Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§ 201 through 219.
(Prior Code, § 35.031) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.032 PERFORMANCE REVIEW.
   (A)   All employees shall be subject to performance evaluations. Such
employee evaluations shall be performed by the department head. Evaluations
will be made twice per year and be included as a permanent part of the
employee’s personnel record. Review of evaluations will be used when issues of
promotion, salary increases, or layoffs are considered.
   (B)   In the event that an area reviewed reveals an employees performance is
below average, such performance should be brought to his or her attention by a
written memo or interview. Such memo or notification of below average
performance will be included in the personnel record.
(Prior Code, § 35.032) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.033 PERSONNEL RECORD.
   A personnel record shall be maintained for each employee by the Clerk-
Treasurer. Documents to be included but not limited to are: application forms,
leaves of absence, vacation and sick leave, charges against vacation/sick
leave, disciplinary actions, and evaluations. Disciplinary actions will be
retained in the personnel record until that point in time at which no new
disciplinary actions have been entered for two years. Dismissal notices shall
become a permanent part of the personnel file.
(Prior Code, § 35.033) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.034 WEEKLY TIME SHEETS.
   (A)   All employees must complete a weekly time sheet to account for work
done, specified by department. No checks will be issued without time sheets.
   (B)   Weekly time sheets are the responsibility of the department head and
shall be submitted to the Clerk-Treasurer by 9:00 a.m. on Monday. All time
sheets shall be verified by the department head and reviewed by the Clerk-
Treasurer. The Clerk-Treasurer is responsible for record keeping, computation,
and payment. In order to comply with federal/state regulations, the following
procedures shall apply to hourly employees.
      (1)   No change in work hours will be allowed without authorization from
the supervisor.
      (2)   Unless specifically authorized by the supervisor, employees who
remain at their work station during lunch hour will not be able to consider
that as extra hours worked.
      (3)   Pay periods are bi-weekly.
(Prior Code, § 35.034) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.035 AUTHORITY AND RESPONSIBILITY.
   Employees of the town (except employees of the Clerk-Treasurer’s office who
are responsible to the Clerk-Treasurer) are responsible to the appropriate
department head and the Council. Questions, inquires, or complaints concerning
work-related activities should be directed first to the department head. If the
employee does not receive a satisfactory answer, a meeting with the Council may
be requested. Failure to follow this chain-of-command will result in
disciplinary action.
(Prior Code, § 35.035) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.036 VALIDITY OF AN ORDER.
   If an employee questions the validity of an order, he or she shall be
expected to complete the task assigned provided the order is not in violation
of the law or safety regulations. If an employee shall have any grievances, he
or she shall follow the grievances procedure.
(Prior Code, § 35.036) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
Cross-reference:
   Grievances procedure, see §§
33.097 and
33.098
   Layoff policy, see §
33.050
   Seniority, see §
33.047
§ 33.037 EMPLOYEE APPEARANCE; CLOTHING ALLOWANCE.
   (A)   All employees will attire themselves in a manner that is conducive to
their work and which does not jeopardize the safety of themselves or other
employees. No employee will report to work if the employee’s health and/or
hygiene jeopardizes the health of other employees.
(Prior Code, § 35.037)
   (B)   The town has allotted up to $700 per year to be paid quarterly in the
amount of up to $175 per quarter per employee as reimbursements pursuant to
this section.
   (C)   The employee will be responsible for the following:
      (1)   Uniform shirts, must have employee name;
      (2)   Uniform pants, may be denim jeans;
      (3)   Uniform shorts, must be neat and tasteful in appearance.
         (a)   No cut-off shorts, short shorts, spandex, or bike shorts.
         (b)   Electrical employees are not authorized to wear shorts.
      (4)   Outerwear;
      (5)   Safety footwear, must be approved for the particular utility;
      (6)   Employee must wear at least one article of clothing that identifies
them as a utility employee.
   (D)   If IOSHA requires certain types of clothing in the above list, IOSHA
rules will be followed. Department supervisors will be responsible to ensure
that IOSHA rules pertaining to clothing and equipment will be adhered to.
   (E)   The town will provide special related safety items that are used to
perform certain job related tasks (e.g., specific gloves to perform a specific
task). The Council will decide if an item would be considered a special safety
item.
   (F)   The Internal Revenue Service provides that clothing and uniforms are
excluded from wages if the following two conditions are met:
      (1)   The clothing is specifically required as a condition of employment;
and
      (2)   The clothing is not worn or adaptable to general usage as ordinary
clothing.
   (G)   The town finds that the above list of clothing is specifically
required as a condition of employment for the town’s utility employees and
instructs employees not to wear or adapt the same to general usage as ordinary
clothing.
   (H)   In addition, the employee shall substantiate his or her use of the
clothing allowance by providing the Clerk-Treasurer with receipts for his or
her purchases pursuant to this policy and pursuant to a voucher form created by
the Clerk-Treasurer no later than December 15 of each year. Any amounts for
which a receipt is not submitted prior to December 15 of the year of the
purchase shall not be reimbursable. An employee shall be required to present
such receipts to the Clerk-Treasurer prior to receiving or becoming eligible
for any reimbursement pursuant to this policy. Items purchased that are
required by IOSHA will be so marked.
   (I)   Where IOSHA requires an item of safety clothing for employees and the
allowance provided in this section is not sufficient to meet the requirement,
the employee shall notify the town that he or she was unable to purchase the
IOSHA-required safety equipment due to insufficient clothing allowance funds,
and the town shall provide the IOSHA-required safety equipment.
   (J)   A copy of this policy will be given to each utility employee, and the
employee will sign and date an affirmation stating that he or she has received
this policy.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992; Res. 14-100, passed 3-10-2014)
§ 33.038 EMPLOYEE TARDINESS.
   (A)   Employees who are tardy two days within a three-month period shall
receive a warning letter from their supervisors. Further tardiness will be met
with suspension or dismissal.
   (B)   Any employee finding that he or she will have to be absent or tardy on
a regular working day shall notify the supervisor at least one hour before the
beginning of the work period. Failure to do so will be an unauthorized absence
and will result in loss of pay.
(Prior Code, § 35.038) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.039 UNAUTHORIZED ABSENCE.
   Employees absent without authorized leave shall not be paid for such
absence. Unauthorized absence for any length of time shall be cause for
disciplinary action, including suspension or termination, to be determined by
the supervisor or Council.
(Prior Code, § 35.039) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.040 EMPLOYEE INTOXICATION.
   Intoxication of an employee on the job due to alcohol, narcotics, or other
substances causing impairment to the employee’s judgement, mental process, and/
or physical ability will not be tolerated. Any employee considered by his or
her supervisor to be intoxicated will be subject to immediate disciplinary
action including automatic suspension and possible termination. Alcoholic
beverages or narcotic substances are strictly forbidden on town property.
(Prior Code, § 35.040) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.041 MUNICIPAL VEHICLES AND DISTRACTED DRIVING.
   (A)   The town sets forth the requirements and expectations that those who
operate a municipal vehicle for any purpose will not engage in activities that
constitute distracted driving. Violations of this policy will be subject to
discipline up to and including termination.
   (B)   While operating a municipal vehicle for any purpose, the driver shall
maintain a proper lookout to see or hear what should be seen or heard and
refrain from engaging in any non-driving activity that has the potential to
district the driver from the primary task of driving. Use of a
telecommunications device to type, transmit, or read text messages or
electronic mail messages is expressly prohibited.
   (C)   This policy shall be implemented in the following manner.
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to all fiscal
officers of the town.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer for public inspection.
      (3)   Any other such action that would communicate the policies
established herein to the employees and elected or appointed officials of the
town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (4)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
(Res. 17-103, passed 6-12-2017)
§ 33.042 COMMISSION OF A FELONY OR UNLAWFUL ACT.
   Any employee convicted of a felony in relation to his or her job is subject
to immediate disciplinary action.
(Prior Code, § 35.041) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.043 CONFLICT OF INTEREST.
   Employees having financial interest in a company which might benefit from
their dealings with the town must file a conflict of interest statement with
the appropriate authorities. Failure to do so may result in dismissal from
municipal employment.
(Prior Code, § 35.042) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.044 OUTSIDE EMPLOYMENT.
   Employees of the town may have part-time jobs as long as the “second job”
does not interfere or conflict with the work schedule as a town employee.
Employees of the town who work in positions where operations are on a
continuous basis (shift operations) should realize they may be required to work
any shift deemed necessary as directed by their department head or supervisor.
(Prior Code, § 35.043) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.045 OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND SAFETY.
   It is the town’s policy to provide safe working conditions for all
employees. It is the obligation of each employee to observe safety regulations
at all times. Failure to observe safety regulation may result in disciplinary
action.
(Prior Code, § 35.044) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.046 REPORTING ACCIDENTS; TREATMENT OF INJURIES.
   (A)   All accidents and injuries shall be reported immediately to the
department head and the Council. Personnel injured in the performance of duties
for the town shall be paid for time spent enroute to receive medical attention.
Eligibility for pay shall cease when the employee is released by the attending
physician unless the employee returns immediately to work and is able to
accomplish normal tasks of that work day.
   (B)   Employees who receive medical attention by a qualified physician and
are not physically able to return to work shall be considered on workers’
compensation. The employee must furnish a statement from the physician which
includes the nature of the injury and estimated date for return to work. The
employee may go to the physician of his or her choosing; in an emergency
situation, the employee will be taken to the emergency room of the hospital or
to the Town Clinic.
(Prior Code, § 35.045) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.047 SENIORITY.
   An employee of the town will accrue seniority through continuous
departmental employment with the town. Seniority will be considered for
promotions, layoffs, hiring, and pay raises.
(Prior Code, § 35.046) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.048 TAKE-HOME VEHICLES.
   (A)   The town sets forth the following restrictions on employee use of
municipal vehicles. Violations of this section will be subject to discipline up
to and including termination.
   (B)   With the exception of the Town Marshal’s vehicle and approved
municipal utility vehicles of on-call employees, no employee shall be permitted
to take home any vehicle owned or leased by the town.
      (1)   Vehicles owned or leased by the town may be utilized by employees
during work hours only.
      (2)   No employee shall utilize a vehicle owned or leased by the town to
travel home on their lunch hour. All vehicles owned or leased by the town
should be parked, and employees should use their personal vehicles to travel
home at lunch time.
   (C)   The resolution codified herein shall be implemented in the following
manner.
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to all fiscal
officers of the town.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer for public inspection.
      (3)   Any other such action that would communicate the policies
established in the resolution codified herein to the employees and elected or
appointed officials of the town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (4)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
   (D)   The utility vehicle will be provided only for weekends and holidays
for the employee on call.
      (1)   The vehicle will be used only for the coming and going of utility
business. The vehicle will not be used for personal use.
      (2)   No unauthorized person will be in a Utility Department vehicle.
      (3)   The Council will rescind this division (D) in its entirety or to
the individual that may violate any guidelines set forth by the Council.
(Res. 15-103, passed 12-14-2015; Res. 17-104, passed 6-12-2017)
§ 33.049 DRUGS AND ALCOHOL POLICY.
   (A)   It is a violation of the policy of the town for any employee,
regardless of the department, office, or classification of employment, to:
      (1)   Manufacture, distribute, dispense, possess, use, or be present
under the influence of any alcoholic beverage, malt beverage, wine, or
intoxicating liquor; or
      (2)   Unlawfully manufacture, distribute, dispense, possess, use, or be
under the influence of any controlled substance as defined under state law
(other than a drug taken as prescribed specifically for the employee by a
physician under a valid and current written prescription), before, during, or
after working hours while on town-owned property, in any town-owned vehicle, or
off town- owned premises while engaged in any activity in the course of the
employee’s employment by the town.
(Prior Code, § 35.105)
   (B)   Any employee of the town engaged directly or indirectly in work for
which the town is a recipient of a grant, including any participatory
agreement, from or with any federal agency, is further notified that he or she
must comply with the following conditions.
      (1)   Adherence to the town’s drug and alcohol policy is a condition of
continued employment under such grant or agreement.
      (2)   Each employee under any federal grant or agreement is required to
notify the Council in writing within five days of the employee’s conviction of
any criminal drug offense for any activity which occurs in violation of the
town’s drug and alcohol policy.
(Prior Code, § 35.106)
   (C)   Any employee of the town who violates the terms of the town’s drug and
alcohol policy shall be subject to immediate disciplinary action ranging from a
written warning to termination from employment, as the Council in its
discretion may order. In addition, upon receipt of notification of a violation
by any employee, the Council may require the employee to participate
satisfactorily in a drug abuse assistance or rehabilitation program approved
for such purposes by federal, state, local health, law enforcement, or other
appropriate agency.
(Prior Code, § 35.107)
(Ord. 232-96, passed 2-12-1996)
§ 33.050 LAYOFF POLICY.
   (A)   The method for determining which employees will be laid off first
shall be based on the following conditions in a particular job category:
      (1)   Length of service in department; and
      (2)   The relative efficiency of the work of the employee.
   (B)   Ten calendar days’ notice will be given before layoffs whenever
possible. The responsibility for administering layoff procedures shall rest
with the Council.
(Prior Code, § 35.047) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
EMPLOYMENT BENEFITS
§ 33.065 PAID HOLIDAYS.
   (A)   All full-time employees will receive the following paid holidays per
year with the exception of the Police Department:
      (1)   New Year’s Day;
      (2)   Martin Luther King Jr. Day;
      (3)   President’s Day;
      (4)   Good Friday;
      (5)   Memorial Day;
      (6)   Independence Day;
      (7)   Labor Day;
      (8)   Veteran’s Day;
      (9)   Thanksgiving Day; and
      (10)   Christmas Day.
   (B)   In addition to days listed, other days or part of a day may be
designated by the Council as a holiday. The additions will be documented by a
resolution or ordinance.
(Prior Code, § 35.060)
   (C)   When any enumerated holiday listed in division (A) above falls on
Sunday and is observed by the state and/or federal government the following
day, the holiday will be observed and paid on Monday. When a holiday falls on
Saturday, the holiday will be observed and paid as holiday on Friday. To be
eligible, an employee must have physically worked the scheduled work day prior
to and following the holiday or be on approved vacation or personal days.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992; Ord. 219-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 227-2-95,
passed 7-18-1995; Ord. 234-96, passed 5-13-1996; Ord. 100-04, passed 1-12-2004;
Ord. 106-24; passed 11-12-2024)
§ 33.066 VACATION LEAVE.
   (A)   The amount of vacation to which an eligible employee shall be entitled
will be based on the employee’s length of employment with the town. A full-time
employee with one or more years of employment shall be entitled to vacation as
follows:
 
Length of Employment for Full-Time Employees Allotted Vacation Time
Employed 1 year but less than 3 years        1 week of vacation
Employed 3 years but less than 10 years      2 weeks of vacation
Employed 10 years but less than 20 years     3 weeks of vacation
Employed 20 or more years                    4 weeks of vacation
 
   (B)   The anniversary-date procedure will be followed, and employees will
plan vacations with their supervisors so that due consideration to the needs of
the town will be given.
      (1)   Vacation may be taken in part or in full but must be taken within
the year following the year in which it was earned.
      (2)   A vacation request for a specific time can be denied if it would be
detrimental to the operation of the department.
      (3)   Accrued vacation leave is an asset of the employee, and pay for
this time shall be made to the employee’s estate upon resignation, termination,
or death.
      (4)   Part-time or seasonal employees are ineligible for vacation leave
with pay. However, if agreeable with their immediate supervisors, they may take
nonpaid leave when necessary.
      (5)   In calculating vacation leave, the employee’s starting date with
the town will be used as long as the service has been continuous.
(Prior Code, § 35.061)
   (C)   Vacation time will not be carried over to the following year, and no
pay will be issued for vacation time not used by the end of the year. Comp time
is to be used prior to vacation days and personal days.
Hourly Schedules
Department Hours Lunch
Hourly Schedules
Department        Hours                             Lunch
Utility personnel 7:00 a.m. through 3:30 p.m.       30 minutes unpaid lunch
Town Hall office  9:00 a.m. through 5:00 p.m. daily 1 hour unpaid lunch
Police
1st shift         8:00 a.m. through 4:00 p.m.       1 hour unpaid lunch
2nd shift         4:00 p.m. through midnight
3rd shift         Midnight through 8:00 a.m.
 
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992; Ord. 106-24; passed 11-12-2024)
§ 33.067 PERSONAL DAYS; SICK LEAVE.
   (A)   All full-time employees shall receive five personal days with pay.
      (1)   Personal days will be distributed equally over four quarters.
Personal days will be lost if not taken by the end of the year.
      (2)   Upon terminating employment with the town, personal days shall be
prorated according to the number of months employed for the year.
   (B)   An employee off sick for three days or more must have a doctor’s
statement to return to work.
   (C)   Paid sick leave, if approved by the Council, will be paid to all full-
time employees for hospital stays and recuperation periods for a maximum of 30
working days or six weeks per calendar year per occurrence.
      (1)   Sick pay will be 80% of the employees regular pay of which $150 per
week is expended through the insurance company and the town compensating the
amount beyond the $150 to equal 80% of the employee’s weekly wage.
      (2)   Comp time is to be used prior to personal and vacation days (in
that order).
      (3)   To alleviate payroll issues, an employee on sick leave will receive
his or her pay through the town payroll. The insurance checks will be signed
for release and deposited into the fund that payroll was charged.
(Prior Code, § 35.062) (Ord. 100-04, passed 1-12-2004; Ord. 106-24; passed 11-
12-2024)
§ 33.068 COMPENSATORY TIME; STANDBY.
   (A)   Authorized time off with pay for employees not eligible for overtime
pay is to be used for the purpose of compensating employees for extra hours
worked in addition to their assigned work schedule.
      (1)   Compensatory time off must have prior approval of the department
head and the Town Supervisor.
      (2)   All employees except sworn full-time police officers shall receive
compensation as mandated by the Fair Labor Standards Act, being 29 U.S.C. §§
201 through 219, in conjunction with “Compensatory Time Off and Gap Time”.
      (3)   Sworn full-time police officers shall follow the schedule below.
         (a)   Any compensation time accumulated and taken within the same 28-
day cycle shall be exchanged at a one-hour-for-one-hour rate.
         (b)   For any 28-day cycle, hours worked in excess of 160 and less
than or equal to 171 hours shall be granted compensatory time of at the rate of
1:1.
         (c)   For any 28-day cycle, hours worked in excess of 171 hours shall
be awarded reimbursement in accordance with the Fair Labor Standards Act and
any “Compensatory Time Off” agreements between the town and the employee.
         (d)   All compensatory time off must have prior approval of the Town
Marshal and the Council.
(Prior Code, § 35.063)
   (B)   Utility employees shall receive comp time as overtime.
   (C)   Stand-by time will be on weekly rotation to a different employee each
week. If an employee cannot be on call or wear his or her pager during this
period, the employee shall forfeit the stand-by credit unless an exchange
agreement is worked out and approved by the Council prior to that week.
   (D)   Compensation time will be given as one-and-a-half time for each hour
worked over, following 40 hours of physically worked time. Personal days,
vacation time, and holidays are not considered physically worked time.
   (E)   All comp time is to be initialed and detailed by the employee and his
or her supervisor. No comp time will be calculated for periods of less than 15
hours over following an actual 40-hour work week.
   (F)   Compt time is to be used prior to vacation and personal days. Compt
time is required as soon as possible, and only December may be carried over
because of weather.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992; Ord. 106-24, passed 11-12-2024)
§ 33.069 PROFESSIONAL LEAVE.
   Authorized leave with pay for employees to attend conferences, workshops,
conventions, or other appropriate activities which will further his or her
effectiveness on the job. Authorization will come from the Council and must be
prior to the activity if the employee is to receive pay. Authorization from the
Council is required if overnight lodging is involved.
(Prior Code, § 35.064) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.070 TRAINING LEAVE.
   Authorized leave or release time for employees to take advantage of training
opportunities which will improve their skills and/or contribute to the town.
The Council shall authorize reimbursement for cost relating to approved
training.
(Prior Code, § 35.065) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.071 BEREAVEMENT LEAVE.
   (A)   Employees may take up to three days of paid time off to attend the
funeral and make any necessary arrangements due to the death of an immediate
family member, except where allowed additional time off for bereavement under
applicable state law.
   (B)   For the purpose of this section, the definition shall apply unless the
context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      IMMEDIATE FAMILY. Consists of the employee’s:
         (a)   Spouse, domestic partner, child, sibling, parents, grandparents,
and grandchildren;
         (b)   The child, siblings, or parents of the employee’s spouse or
domestic partner; and/or
         (c)   Brother-in-law, sister-in-law, mother-in-law, father-in-law, and
grandparents-in-law.
   (C)   Employees must notify their supervisor as soon as possible if they
need to take bereavement leave. Approval of bereavement leave will occur in the
absence of any unusual town operating requirements. Any employee may, with his
or her supervisor’s approval, use any available paid leave for additional time
off beyond three days as necessary.
(Prior Code, § 35.066) (Ord. 106-24; passed 11-12-2024)
§ 33.072 MILITARY LEAVE.
   Any employee who is a member of the Reserve Armed Forces shall be entitled
to a leave of absence without loss of pay for not more than 15 calendar days
annually, provided the employee has submitted notification papers to his or her
supervisor. Upon receipt of military pay voucher, the Clerk-Treasurer will pay
the employee the difference between that amount and the regular pay.
(Prior Code, § 35.067) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.073 JURY LEAVE.
   Jury service may be granted to employees who are required to serve as a
juror or to appear as a witness for the federal, state, or city government. All
employees, when summoned, shall immediately inform the Council. Salary payments
will be reduced by the amount received from the court for jury duty. Payment by
the court to the employee for traveling expenses may be retained by the
employee.
(Prior Code, § 35.068) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.074 MATERNITY LEAVE.
   A full-time employee with one-year continuous service may receive maternity
leave of up to six months without loss of seniority or benefits.
(Prior Code, § 35.069) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.075 UNPAID LEAVE OF ABSENCE.
   (A)   Employees may be granted a personal leave of absence without pay at
the discretion of the Council in consultation with the Town Supervisor. An
employee desiring a leave of absence shall make written application to the Town
Supervisor and receive written approval of the Council before such leave
begins. The application shall state the dates for which leave is requested and
reasons for such leave.
   (B)   Unpaid leaves of absence may be denied if the requested leave
interferes with the efficient operation of the department. All approved leaves
of absence without pay shall sustain loss of seniority and benefits. Leaves of
absence may not be granted for more than six months, after which the employee
may return to his or her former position if available. An employee absent from
work due to accident or illness will be guaranteed the right to return to the
same job provided he or she returns to work within six months from the last day
worked if approved by the Council.
(Prior Code, § 35.070) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.076 INSURANCE.
   Health, hospitalization, and life insurance is available to all full-time
employees and qualified dependents. The employee is eligible to enroll in the
insurance program after 90 days of probation. All premiums related to this
insurance are paid through the town.
(Prior Code, § 35.071) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.077 PUBLIC EMPLOYEES RETIREMENT FUND.
   All full-time employees must participate in PERF when they start employment
with the town.
(Prior Code, § 35.072) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.078 UNEMPLOYMENT COMPENSATION.
   Eligible town employees are covered by the unemployment compensation program
administered through the Employment Security Division of the State Department
of Revenue. Generally, this compensation is available to those who are
terminated from town employment through no fault of their own (layoffs,
cutbacks, and the like) and are actively seeking employment elsewhere.
Eligibility is determined by the Employment Security Division on a case basis.
(Prior Code, § 35.073) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.079 WORKERS’ COMPENSATION INSURANCE.
   All employees covered by workers’ compensation insurance to the extent of
normal legal benefits. The employee has the responsibility of reporting any
accident or injury which occurs to his or her immediate supervisor and the
Council.
(Prior Code, § 35.074) (Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.080 EXPENSES FOR TRAVEL, CONVENTIONS, AND EDUCATION.
   (A)   The town will reimburse employees for authorized mileage, meals, and
lodging upon submission of itemized claim with receipts.
      (1)   Any employee obligated to use his or her own vehicle outside the
boundaries of the town will be reimbursed for mileage and parking tolls.
Mileage sheets should be submitted to the Council for approval and subsequent
payment.
            (2)   Any travel requiring overnight accommodations must be
approved by the Council prior to departure.
(Prior Code, § 35.075)
   (B)   (1)   Any employee that attends conventions or meetings pre-approved
by the Council, with exception of monthly IMPA or mandatory State Board of
Accounts meetings of one or more days, will be reimbursed for meal expenses
with proper receipts if meals are not included in the registration cost.
      (2)   Mileage will be reimbursed at the rate set by the Internal Revenue
Service for business use when a town vehicle is not available for
transportation.
(Prior Code, § 31.02)

of this code of ordinances as fully as if set out at length herein.

DISCIPLINARY ACTIONS; GRIEVANCE PROCEDURES
§ 33.095 EMPLOYEES SUBJECT TO DISCIPLINARY ACTIONS.
   Employees are subject to disciplinary action as a result of a violation of
rules, regulations, or policies as determined by the supervisor and Council.
Employees of the Marshal’s Department are also subject to the rules and
regulations of that department. Such disciplinary action may include, but will
not be limited to, the following procedure:
   (A)   First offense. Written reprimand;
   (B)   Second offense. One-day suspension without pay;
   (C)   Third offense. Three-day suspension without pay;
   (D)   Fourth offense. Subject to discharge.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)

§ 33.097 GRIEVANCE POLICY.
   (A)   It is the policy of the town to treat all employees equitably and
fairly in matters affecting their employment. Each employee of the town will be
provided ample opportunity to understand and resolve matters affecting his or
her employment which the employee believes are unjust. Grievance procedure
instructions shall be made available to each employee, and the presentation of
any grievance shall be considered the right of each employee without fear of
reprisal.
   (B)   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning. A
GRIEVANCE is defined as:
      (1)   Alleged poor working conditions;
      (2)   The unjust application of discipline; and/or
      (3)   The unfair application, interpretation, or violation of the town’s
uniform rules and regulations or those of the department in which the employee
works.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.098 GRIEVANCE PROCEDURE.
   The following steps are the approved grievance procedures for employees of
the town.
   (A)   Step 1. An employee who feels aggrieved should first discuss the
grievance with their supervisor. If the employee does not obtain satisfaction,
he or she should proceed with the next step.
   (B)   Step 2. An employee wishing further appeal may request a meeting in
executive session with the Council. Such a request must be in writing and be
submitted to the Clerk-Treasurer within five days of the receipt of the written
decision of the Town Supervisor. The decision of the Council is final.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)
§ 33.099 SEXUAL/UNLAWFUL HARASSMENT.
   (A)   The town sets forth the position and policy of the town regarding
sexual and other unlawful harassment. Violations of this section will be
subject to discipline up to and including termination.
   (B)   The town is committed to providing a workplace free of:
      (1)   Sexual and sex-based harassment, intimidation, threats, coercion,
or discrimination (which includes harassment based on gender, pregnancy,
childbirth, or related medical conditions); and
      (2)   Harassment, intimidation, threats, coercion, or discrimination
based on such factors as race, color, religion, national origin, ancestry, age,
physical disability, mental disability, medical condition, marital status,
sexual orientation, family-care leave status, genetic information, or veteran
status, without limitation.
   (C)   The town strongly disapproves of and will not tolerate harassment of
employees by managers, supervisors, or coworkers. Similarly, the town will not
tolerate harassment by its employees of non-employees with whom the town has a
business, service, or professional relationship.
   (D)   HARASSMENT includes written, verbal, physical, and visual conduct that
creates an intimidating, offensive, or hostile work environment or that
interferes with work performance.
      (1)   Such conduct constitutes HARASSMENT when:
         (a)   Submission to the conduct is made either an explicit or implicit
condition of employment;
         (b)   Submission to or rejection of the conduct is used as the basis
for an employment decision; or
         (c)   The harassment interferes with an employee’s work performance or
creates an intimidating, hostile, or offensive work environment.
      (2)   HARASSING CONDUCT can take many forms and includes but is not
limited to: slurs, jokes, statements, gestures, pictures, or cartoons regarding
an employee’s sex, race, color, national origin, religion, age, physical
disability, mental disability, medical condition, ancestry, marital status,
sexual orientation, family-care leave status, or veteran status.
      (3)   SEXUALLY HARASSING CONDUCT in particular includes all of the
prohibited actions in this division as well as other unwelcome conduct such as
requests for sexual favors, conversation containing sexual comments, and
unwelcome sexual advances.
      (4)   Such conduct is absolutely prohibited by the town. It is prohibited
by federal and state statutes, regulations, and guidelines. Sexual harassment
undermines the integrity of the employment relationship and will not be
tolerated in any form.
   (E)   Complaints will be investigated promptly and in a confidential manner
to the extent practical. When, after investigation, the town has reason to
believe that an employee did in fact violate the town’s policy prohibiting
sexual or other unlawful harassment, the town will immediately take appropriate
disciplinary action to end the harassment and to prevent the misconduct from
recurring. Sexual and other unlawful harassment will be grounds for immediate
discipline up to and including termination of employment.
      (1)   All actions taken to investigate and resolve sexual and other
unlawful harassment complaints shall be conducted in such a manner as to
respect all parties’ privacy. The identities of the complaining party and the
person accused of the harassment will be kept confidential to the extent
possible.
      (2)   There will be no retaliation or discrimination against any employee
for making a sexual and other unlawful harassment complaint. The town will make
follow-up inquiries to ensure that the harassment has not resumed and to
prevent retaliation from being taken.
      (3)   False accusations of harassment will not be tolerated. Given the
nature of this type of discrimination, everyone must also recognize that false
accusations of harassment can have serious effects on innocent individuals.
      (4)   Should an employee be found to have engaged in a violation of this
policy or knowingly provide a false charge, he or she will be subject to
discipline up to and including termination of employment.
   (F)   It is the responsibility of all management personnel at all levels,
including those people serving temporarily as supervisors, to enforce the
town’s sexual and other unlawful harassment policy. Every supervisor has an
affirmative duty to maintain a work environment free from sexual and other
unlawful harassment. Management personnel who fail to enforce this policy
strictly and promptly may also be subject to disciplinary action up to and
including termination.
   (G)   There shall be no express time limits for initiating complaints for
sexual or other unlawful harassment. However, every effort should be made to
file the complaint as soon as possible, preferably within 48 working hours
after any incident occurs, while the facts and potential supporting witness
statements are readily available.
   (H)   The following procedure shall apply in initiating a sexual harassment
complaint:
      (1)   Any person who is aggrieved by any form of sexual harassment, who
feels comfortable doing so, should directly inform the offending person that
the conduct is offensive and must stop.
      (2)   If the aggrieved person does not wish to communicate directly with
the offending person, or if the direct communication with the offending person
is not successful, the aggrieved person shall contact the offending person’s
immediate supervisor or Human Resources. If the complaint involves someone in
the employee’s direct line of supervision, then the employee should inform
another supervisor of the complaint.
      (3)   All complaints of sexual harassment will be put in writing and
promptly investigated by the town for appropriate remedial action, which may
include counseling, written warning, demotion, suspension, termination, or any
other appropriate action.
   (I)   This policy shall be implemented in the following manner.
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to all fiscal
officers of the town.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer of the town for public inspection.
      (3)   Any other such action that would communicate the policies
established herein to the employees and elected or appointed officials of the
town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (4)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
(Res. 17-102, passed 6-12-2017)
§ 33.100 TERMINATION.
   All nonprobationary employees terminated may have an exit interview with the
Council in executive session. Notices of termination will be approved by the
Town Supervisor and reviewed by the Council. Keys and any property belonging to
the town will be turned in before the Clerk-Treasurer will issue the final
paychecks.
(Ord. 207-92, passed 7-13-1992)

   The town has hereby established an Ordinance Violations Bureau for the town
and appointed the Clerk-Treasurer as the Clerk of the Ordinance Violations
Bureau to perform all duties allowed under I.C. 33-36-2-3.
(Ord. 102-15, passed 2-9-2015)
§ 34.02 CONFLICTS.
   The general and specific penalty sections of the town’s code shall remain in
effect, except where a specific penalty set forth herein conflicts with the
specific or general penalty sections of the town’s code, in which case the
penalty set forth herein amends such conflicting specific or general penalty
section of the town’s code.
(Ord. 102-15, passed 2-9-2015)

§ 34.04 MULTIPLE VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   Any person, corporation, individual, or organization who commits more
than one violation of any of the offenses specifically noted herein within any
30-day period will be subject to an increased fine as set forth above for
second or subsequent violations.
   (B)   Every day or part of a day that a violation is committed or continues
in existence is a separate violation for which civil penalties may be imposed.
(Ord. 102-15, passed 2-9-2015)
§ 34.05 PAYMENT PROCEDURE; MAXIMUM AMOUNTS.
   (A)   The Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau shall accept written
appearances, waivers of trial, admissions of violations, and payment of civil
penalties up to but not exceeding $250 in ordinance violation cases.
   (B)   Civil penalties shall be paid to, receipted by, and accounted for by
the Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau under procedures provided by the
State Board of Accounts. Payment of such civil penalties may be made in person
or by mail.
   (C)   All sums collected by the Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau as
civil penalties for ordinance violations shall be accounted for and paid to the
town as provided by law.
(Ord. 102-15, passed 2-9-2015)
§ 34.06 RIGHT TO TRIAL.
   (A)   (1)   A person, corporation, individual, or organization charged with
an ordinance violation is entitled to a trial before a court as provided by law
unless the person, corporation, individual, or organization waives the right to
trial and enters an admission of the violation with the Clerk of the Ordinance
Violations Bureau.
      (2)   Upon admission, the Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau shall
assess and receive from the violator the amount prescribed by the schedule
provided in §
34.03. If a person, corporation, individual, or organization charged with a
violation wishes to exercise the right to trial, that person shall appear
before the Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau and deny the violation or
enter a written denial with the Clerk.
   (B)   If a person, corporation, individual, or organization denies an
ordinance or code violation; fails to satisfy a civil penalty assessed by the
Clerk of the Ordinance Violations Bureau after having entered an admission of
violation; or fails to deny or admit such a violation, the Clerk of the
Ordinance Violations Bureau shall report this fact to the Town Attorney who
shall then initiate proceedings against the person, corporation, individual, or
organization for the alleged ordinance violation.
(Ord. 102-15, passed 2-9-2015)

§ 35.01 MUNICIPAL ELECTIONS.
   (A)   The Council provides that its members are to be elected at large by
the voters of the whole town.
(Prior Code, § 30.01)
   (B)   The town will conduct its own municipal elections at one general
polling place, being the Town Hall, as provided by I.C. 3-10-7-22.
(Res. 07-102, passed 7-9-2007)
Statutory reference:

Indiana — Seal”.
(Prior Code, § 30.02)
Statutory reference:

§ 35.03 FRANKTON DAY.
   The Council designates December 4 as Frankton Day. The citizens of the town
are encouraged to recognize and celebrate the anniversary of the incorporation

(Prior Code, § 36.01)
