§ 35.04 PARTICIPATION IN REGISTERED CITIES AND TOWNS PROGRAM.
   The town pledges its full support and cooperation to the Registered Cities
and Towns Program.
(Prior Code, § 36.02) (Res. 85-64, passed 10-14-1985)
§ 35.05 NONDISCRIMINATION POLICY.
   (A)   It is the policy of the town that no discrimination on the basis of
race, religion, age, color, sex, national origin, or ancestry be condoned or
permitted against any person or persons in the recruitment or hiring of
employees, in any personnel transaction, in the provision of government
services, in the accessibility of town programs and facilities, in the
solicitation of bids, or the award of public contracts.
   (B)   All complaints of discrimination against any officer or personnel of
town shall be referred to the Council, which shall promptly make orders
appropriate to the circumstances of such case.
(Prior Code, § 36.03) (Ord. 223-96, passed 2-12-1996)
§ 35.06 TITLE VI PLAN.
   The Title VI Implementation Plan is adopted and incorporated as part of this
code as fully as if set out at length herein.
(Res. 20-102, passed 4-13-2020)
§ 35.07 ADA COMPLIANCE.
   (A)   Transition Plan. The Americans with Disabilities Act Transition Plan
is adopted and incorporated as part of this code of ordinances as fully as if
set out at length herein.
      (1)   The plan is hereby approved and adopted to be utilized as a guide
for moving the town toward compliance of the required ADA standards and
regulations.
      (2)   The town will fully consider the schedule of modifications
contained therein and will direct its consultant to provide amended copies of
the plan reflecting modifications to be made in the near future.
   (B)   Employment. The town does not discriminate on the basis of disability
in its hiring or employment practices and complies with all regulations as
outlined by the U.S. Equal Employment Opportunity Commission under Title 1 of
the (ADA).
   (C)   Effective communication. The town will, upon request, provide
appropriate aids and services leading to effective participation for people
with disabilities to participate equally in the town’s programs, services, and
activities. Anyone who requires an auxiliary aid or service for effective
participation or modification of policies or procedures to participate in a
service, program, or activity should contact the office of the Clerk-Treasurer
as soon as possible, but no later than 48 hours before the scheduled event.
   (D)   Modification to policies and procedures. The town will make all
reasonable modifications to policies and programs to ensure that people with
disabilities have an equal opportunity to enjoy all of its programs, services,
and activities. The ADA does not require the town to take any action that would
fundamentally alter the nature of its services or programs or impose an undue
financial or administrative burden to the town.
      (1)   Grievances regarding a service, program, or activity of the town
that is not accessible to persons with disabilities should be directed to the
Clerk-Treasurer and use the appropriate grievance procedure form.
      (2)   The town will not place a surcharge on a particular individual with
a disability or any group of individuals with disabilities to cover the cost of
providing auxiliary aids/services or reasonable modifications of policy, such
as retrieving items from locations that are open to the public but are not
accessible to persons who use wheelchairs.
   (E)   Procedures. The town has adopted an internal grievance procedure
providing for prompt and equitable resolution of complaints alleging any action
prohibited by the U.S. Department of Justice regulations implementing Title II
of the ADA. Title II states, in part, that “no otherwise qualified disabled
individual shall, solely by reason of such disability, be excluded from the
participation in, be denied the benefits of, or be subjected to discrimination
in programs, services, or activities sponsored by a public entity”.
      (1)   Step one: File the grievance. Complete the state’s Americans with
Disabilities Act Title II Grievance Form.
         (a)   The grievance form can be found on ada.gov or in the ADA
Coordinator’s office located in the Town Hall. A grievance may be communicated
in writing, by e-mail, by fax, or by telephone, but must follow the format of
the state’s Americans with Disabilities Act Title II grievance form.
         (b)   A grievance concerning the accessibility of town services,
programs, or activities should be addressed to “Frankton Clerk-Treasurer, ADA
Coordinator”.
      (2)   Step two: Acknowledgment. A grievance should be filed within 90
days after the grievant party becomes aware of the alleged violation. The ADA
Coordinator will send an acknowledgment of receipt of the grievance within 12
working days.
      (3)   Step three: Informal resolution. Following the filing of a
grievance, the ADA Coordinator shall determine whether, and to what extent, an
investigation of the grievance is warranted.
         (a)   Any resulting investigation shall be conducted by the ADA
Coordinator or his or her designee. A thorough investigation affords all
interested persons and their representatives an opportunity to submit evidence
relevant to a grievance.
         (b)   The ADA Coordinator will complete the investigation within 60
calendar days of receipt of the grievance.
         (c)   If appropriate, the ADA Coordinator will arrange to meet with
the grievant to discuss the matter and attempt to reach an informal resolution
of the grievance. Any informal resolution of the grievance shall be documented
in the ADA Coordinator file and the case will be closed.
      (4)   Step four: Written determination. If an informal resolution of the
grievance is not reached in Step 3, within 60 calendar days of receipt of the
grievance, a written determination as to the validity of the complaint and
description of the resolution, if appropriate, shall be forwarded by the ADA
Coordinator to the Executive Officer for approval.
      (5)   Step five: Final determination and resolution. The ADA Coordinator
shall communicate the determination and resolution to the grievant within 90
calendar days of receipt of the grievance, unless the Executive Officer
authorizes additional time for further consideration of the grievance. Any
authorized extension of time will be communicated to the grievant. Any request
for reconsideration of the response to the grievance shall be at the discretion
of the Executive Officer.
   (F)   Pursuit of other remedies. If the grievant is not satisfied with the
town’s handling of the grievance at any stage of the process or does not wish
to file a grievance through the town’s ADA Title II grievance procedures, the
grievant may file a complaint directly with the U.S. Department of Justice or
other appropriate state or federal agency. Use of the town’s grievance
procedure is not a prerequisite to the pursuit of other remedies.
   (G)   Resolution considerations. The resolution of any specific grievance
will require consideration of varying circumstances such as the specific nature
of the disability; the nature of the access to services, programs, or
facilities at issue; the essential eligibility requirements for participation;
the health and safety of others; and the degree to which an accommodation would
constitute a fundamental alteration to the service, program, or facility or
cause an undue hardship to the town. Accordingly, the resolution by the town of
any one grievance does not constitute a precedent upon which the town is bound
or upon which other complaining parties may rely.
   (H)   File maintenance. The Clerk-Treasurer ADA Coordinator shall maintain
ADA Grievance files for three years.
(Ord. 113-12, passed - -; Res. 13-101, passed 2-11-2013)
§ 35.08 ANTI-NEPOTISM.
   (A)   Policy summary. It shall be the policy of the town to take all
reasonable steps to identify and prevent violations of I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al.
(Nepotism) and I.C. 36-1-21 et al. (Contracting with a Unit). In order to carry
out such policy, the town hereby adopts the following specific policy for
identifying violations of I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al. and I.C. 36-1-21 et al.
   (B)   Identification of violations of the Anti-Nepotism Policy.
      (1)   The town shall maintain records with respect to the direct line of
supervision of each employee of the town and shall prepare documentation of any
familial relationships defined as “relatives” pursuant to I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al.
and 36-1-21 et al. with respect to employees employed by the town on July 1,
2012.
      (2)   Effective July 1, 2012, the town shall require that each new
employee read, complete, and sign the verification of applicant for employment
of compliance with the town’s Anti-Nepotism Policy and shall require employees
to immediately notify the town of any change in information recorded on such
form during their employment with the town.
      (3)   Effective July 1, 2012, prior to the time the town enters into a
contract or renewal of a contract for goods or services or a contract for
public works, the individual or business entity with whom the town is
contracting must certify to the town that the individual, business, or anyone
with an ownership interest in the business, is not a relative of a Council
member as defined by this section and I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al. and I.C. 36-1-21 et
al.
   (D)   Incorporation of I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al. and I.C. 36-1-21 et al. The
town hereby incorporates the minimum requirements and provisions of I.C. 36-1-
20.2 et al. and 36-1-21 et al., including all future supplements and amendments
thereto which become law from time to time, and make them a part hereof as if
fully set out herein.
   (E)   Compliance by elected and appointed officials and employees.
      (1)   The town finds that no single member of the Council can act for the
body to make work assignments, compensation, grievances, advancement, or a
performance evaluation without prior authority of a majority of the Council
and, therefore, without such authority by the majority, he or she will not be
in the direct line of supervision of any employee.
(I.C. 36-5-2-9.4)
      (2)   All elected and appointed officials and employees of the town are
hereby directed to cooperate fully in the implementation of the policies
created by the resolution codified herein and shall demonstrate compliance with
the same. Each elected official shall annually, by December 31 of each year,
certify in writing, subject to the penalties for perjury, and on the form
attached to the resolution codified herein, that the officer has not violated
I.C. 36-1-20.2 et al. or 36-1-21 et al.
      (3)   Failure to abide by or cooperate with the implementation,
compliance, and certifications required by this section is a violation of I.C.
36-1-20.2 et al. and I.C. 36-1-21 et al. and may result in the discipline,
including termination, of an employee or a transfer from the direct line of
supervision or other curative action. An elected or appointed official of the
town who fails to abide by or cooperate with the implementation, compliance,
and mandated certifications of this section may be subject to action allowed by
law.
   (F)   Implementation. This policy shall be implemented in the following
manner.
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be posted at the town’s
offices where similar employment posters or other notices to employees are
located.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to each of the
town’s employees and elected and appointed officials.
      (3)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer for public inspection.
      (4)   Any other such action(s) that would communicate the policies
established herein to the employees and elected or appointed officials of the
town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (5)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
(Res. 12-103, passed 6-11-2012)
§ 35.09 LOSSES; SHORTAGES; THEFTS.
   (A)   The Council approves the following policy regarding erroneous or
irregular material variances, losses, shortages, and thefts. The Council shall
take any and all necessary action that may be required to implement such policy
and to review, monitor, and update the policy as necessary.
   (B)   (1)   The town sets a threshold for all erroneous or irregular
variances, losses, shortages, and thefts of town funds and property to be
considered “material” and thus required to be reported to the State Board of
Accounts under I.C. 5-11-1-27 and State Examiner Directive 2015-6.
      (2)   This section outlines the procedures required when a material
variance, loss, shortage, or theft occurs as well as procedures for maintenance
of documentation and resolution of incidents that do not meet the materiality
threshold established by this section.
   (C)   The materiality threshold at which point the town will be required to
report erroneous or irregular variances, losses, shortages, and thefts to the
State Board of Accounts shall be $100.
      (1)   If the erroneous or irregular variance, loss, shortage, or theft is
not cash or a cash equivalent, the value of the item at the time of the
variance, loss, shortage, or theft shall be used to determine whether the
materiality threshold has been met.
      (2)   All erroneous or irregular variances, losses, shortages, or thefts
of $100 or more shall be reported immediately to the State Board of Accounts
according to the procedures established by that agency. In addition, all
erroneous or irregular variances, losses, shortages, or thefts that occur more
than one time in a month and which, in the aggregate, total $100 or more shall
be reported immediately to the State Board of Accounts.
   (D)   The town shall maintain records and documentation concerning erroneous
or irregular variances, losses, shortages, and thefts in accordance with
generally accepted accounting principles and the internal control standards
developed and provided by the State Board of Accounts, as may be amended from
time to time.
   (E)   The town shall investigate all erroneous or irregular variances,
losses, shortages, and thefts regardless of whether they meet the materiality
threshold established by this policy. Upon conclusion of each investigation,
the town shall implement procedures designed to prevent the recurrence of such
incidents and, if applicable, take appropriate disciplinary action against any
employees responsible for the incident.
   (F)   An employee of the town or other public officer who has knowledge of
or reasonable cause to believe that there has been a misappropriation of public
funds or assets of the public office shall immediately send written notice of
the misappropriation to the State Board of Accounts and the Office of the
Madison County Prosecutor.
   (G)   This policy shall be implemented in the following manner.
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to all fiscal
officers of the town.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer of the town for public inspection.
      (3)   Any other such action that would communicate the policies
established herein to the employees and elected or appointed officials of the
town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (4)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
(Res. 18-100, passed 4-9-2018)
§ 35.10 IDENTITY THEFT.
   (A)   The Identity Theft Policy is adopted and incorporated as part of this
code of ordinances as fully as if set out at length herein.
   (B)   The town may take any and all necessary action as may be required to
implement such policy and to review, monitor, and update as necessary the
Identity Theft Policy. Such review shall include at least annually a report to
the Council or a designated committee of the Council regarding the operation of
the Identity Theft Policy and any modifications recommended by the Clerk-
Treasurer.
(Res. 08-103, passed 10-13-2008)
§ 35.11 INTERNAL CONTROL STANDARDS.
   (A)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
      CONTROL ENVIRONMENT. The demonstration of a commitment of the town to:
         (a)   Integrity and ethical values;
         (b)   Oversight of the internal control system;
         (c)   Establish an organization structure that oversees the
operations, reporting and compliance of the objectives of the internal controls
system;
         (d)   Attracting, developing, and retaining competent individuals; and
         (e)   Evaluating performance and holding individuals responsible for
internal control duties and responsibilities.
      CONTROL ACTIVITIES. The town actions and tools established through
policies and procedures that go towards the detection, prevention, or reduction
of identified risks of loss while still providing government operations by
designing control activities, designing information systems, and implementing
policies.
      INFORMATION AND COMMUNICATION. The internal and external communication
needed to support an internal controls system that is valued and used by
receiving quality information, internally communicating quality information,
and externally communicating quality information.
      MONITORING ACTIVITIES. The activities of the town officials to see that
all of the components of the internal control system that meet or exceed the
standards herein are functioning properly by:
         (a)   Establishing and operating monitoring activities and then
evaluating them on an ongoing or periodic basis; and
         (b)   The timely remediation of deficiencies.
      RISK ASSESSMENT. The process that identifies and assesses internal and
external risks and establishes tolerances of the town sufficient to:
         (a)   Enable the identification of risks and the amount of risks
tolerated;
         (b)   Identify, analyze and to risks;
         (c)   Consider the potential for fraud in the risk assessment process;
and
         (d)   Identify, analyze, and respond to significant changes impacting
the internal control system.
   (B)   The town finds that its mission as related to an internal control
system is as follows:
      (1)   Provision of a democratic governmental structure at the grassroots
level;
      (2)   Provision of services as determined through the political process,
including but not limited to police, fire, streets, roads, economic
development, and parks;
      (3)   Promotion of government efficiency, accountability, reliability,
and transparency; and
      (4)   Promotion of safeguards to reduce the risk of loss due to fraud,
waste, abuse, mismanagement, or errors.
   (C)   The town finds that its interrelated and often overlapping objectives
as related to an internal control system are as follows:
      (1)   Operations objectives which involve the ways governmental services
are performed and the performance of those providing governmental services,
including by way of example: budgeting, purchasing, permitting, cash
management, and planning among others;
      (2)   Reporting objectives which involve the filing of financial and non-
financial information to those inside the government and those outside of the
government, including by way of example: filing the annual report, audit and
examination cooperation, filing uniform conflict of interest forms, and the
other filings with any governmental agency or official or information required
to be kept such as an OSHA log and responding to a public records request among
others; and
      (3)   Compliance objectives involve the adherence to law and regulations,
including by way of example: following guidance documents such as the State
Board of Accounts’ manuals, bulletins, directives; the Department of Local
Government Finance’s forms and directions; and other outside-of- government
trainings and documents such as the State Association of Cities and Towns
(IACT) and the State League of Municipal Clerks and Treasurers, among others,
as appropriate for the town.
   (D)   The town adopts and directs the minimum level of internal control
standards and internal procedures for an internal control system that includes
the following five standards to promote government accountability and
transparency as described in the Uniform Internal Control Standards for Indiana
Political Subdivisions guidance document from the Board of Accounts dated
September 2015 and as thereafter modified:
      (1)   Control environment;
      (2)   Risk assessment;
      (3)   Control activities;
      (4)   Information and communication; and
      (5)   Monitoring.
   (E)   The town adopts and directs the following principles in explanation of
the pertinent standards above be followed at all levels of the town government:
      (1)   Control environment.
         (a)   The oversight body and management demonstrate a commitment to
integrity and ethical values;
         (b)   The oversight body oversees the town’s internal control system;
         (c)   Management establishes an organizational structure, assigns
responsibility, and delegates authority to achieve the town’s objectives;
         (d)   Management demonstrates a commitment to attract, develop, and
retain competent individuals; and
         (e)   Management evaluates performance and holds individuals
accountable for their internal control responsibilities.
      (2)   Risk assessment.
         (a)   Management defines objectives clearly to enable the
identification of risks and defines risk tolerances;
         (b)   Management identifies, analyzes, and responds to risk related to
achieving the defined objectives;
         (c)   Management considers the potential for fraud when identifying,
analyzing and responding to risks; and
         (d)   Management identifies, analyzes, and responds to significant
changes that could impact the internal control system.
      (3)   Control activities.
         (a)   Management designs control activities to achieve objectives and
respond to risks;
         (b)   Management designs the town’s information system and related
control activities to achieve objectives and respond to risks; and
         (c)   Management implements control activities through policies.
      (4)   Information and communication.
         (a)   Management uses quality information to achieve the town’s
objectives;
         (b)   Management internally communicates the necessary quality
information to achieve the town’s objectives; and
         (c)   Management externally communicates the necessary quality
information to achieve the town’s objectives.
      (5)   Monitoring.
         (a)   Management establishes and operates monitoring activities to
monitor the internal control system and evaluate the results; and
         (b)   Management remediates and identifies internal control
deficiencies on a timely basis.
   (F)   The town adopts the internal control standards above so as to
establish an effective internal control system for the town through its design,
implementation, and operation.
   (G)   The town directs that the above standards be used to design,
implement, operate, and modify current operations, reporting, and compliance
objectives that will safeguard the assets of the town; promote reliability,
accountability, and transparency of financial and non-financial information;
and to assure compliance with laws and regulations for each office, department,
and personnel (as defined below) for an effective and reasonable internal
control system of the town.
   (H)   The town authorizes the Clerk-Treasurer, Council President, and Police
Chief (Fiscal Committee) together with the advice of the Town Attorney to
review the current internal control systems of the town and adopt a policy for
the future internal control systems, subject to the approval of the Council, to
be effective after June 30, 2016 and perform an annual review or more if
determined necessary for compliance with this section.
   (I)   The personnel, whether an official or employee of the town whose
official duties include receiving, processing, depositing, disbursing, or
otherwise having access to funds that belong to the federal government, state
government, a political subdivision, or another governmental entity shall
comply with these minimum internal control standards and procedures and any
other policy regarding standards and procedures determined necessary by the
town now and as modified in the future.
   (J)   The personnel of the town, both elected and appointed, whose official
duties include receiving, processing, depositing, disbursing, or otherwise
having access to funds that belong to the federal government, state government,
a political subdivision, or another governmental entity shall be trained at
least once during a calendar year and annually thereafter, unless on leave
status, on the minimum internal control standards and procedures and any other
standards and procedures determined necessary by the town and shall cooperate
with the town fiscal officer or designee so that the fiscal officer can timely
certify to the State Board of Accounts that the training was received annually
by the personnel as provided by law.
   (K)   The town authorizes the Committee, subject to the approval of the
Council, to determine the position and person who are the personnel referred to
in divisions (I) and (J) above and notify such personnel.
   (L)   The Council delegates to the Fiscal Committee, subject to Council
approval, the authority and/or duties including creating the internal control
systems policy.
   (M)   All elected and appointed officials and employees of the town are
hereby directed to abide by and to cooperate fully in the implementation of the
internal control system of the town.
   (N)   An employee who fails to abide by or cooperate with the
implementation, compliance, and certifications connected with the internal
control systems commits a violation of and may result in the discipline,
including termination, of the employee.
   (O)   An elected or appointed official of the town who fails to abide by or
cooperate with the implementation and the mandated certifications of the
internal control system may be subject to any action allowed by law.
   (P)   The ordinance codified herein may be implemented by any and all of the
following actions or such others as authorized by this Council:
      (1)   Posting a copy of the ordinance codified herein in its entirety in
at least one of the locations in the town where it posts employee posters or
other notices to its employees;
      (2)   Providing a copy of the ordinance codified herein to its employees
and elected and appointed officials;
      (3)   Providing or posting the adoption of the ordinance codified herein;
or
      (4)   Any such other action or actions that would communicate the
policies established herein this section to its employees and elected and
appointed officials.
(Ord. 103-16, passed 6-13-2016)
§ 35.12 PROCEDURES GOVERNING MEETINGS.
   The Council adopts the following rules of parliamentary procedure to govern
its meetings.
   (A)   The members-elect of the Council shall hold their first regular
meeting on the first Monday in January after their election, at the hour of 7:
30 p.m. local time in the Town Hall. The regular meetings of the Council shall
be held on the second and fourth Monday of each month at 7:30 p.m. local time
in the Town Hall.
   (B)   Any regular meeting of the Council may be adjourned to a future day or
hour. At that adjourned meeting, the Council may transact any business which
might have been transacted at the regular meeting of which it is a
continuation. At the adjourned meeting, the Council shall not be limited to
completing particular items of business which have actually been entered upon
and left unfinished at the regular meeting, unless the order of adjournment of
the regular meeting limited the work of the adjourned meeting.
   (C)   The President of the Council shall preside over all meetings of the
Council. The President of the Council shall take the chair precisely at the
hour fixed for the meeting and shall call the Council to order if a quorum is
present.
   (D)   (1)   The business of the Council shall be taken up in the following
order:
         (a)   Roll call;
         (b)   Reading of the minutes of the last meeting;
         (c)   General committee reports;
         (d)   Public hearing (opportunity for citizens to speak);
         (e)   New business;
         (f)   Unfinished business;
         (g)   Bills and claims; and
         (h)   Adjournment.
      (2)   The order of business may be suspended or altered by a two-thirds
vote of the Council.
   (E)   The President of the Council shall preserve order and decorum in all
Council meetings.
   (F)   The President shall ask for the “yeas” and “nays” on any question
before the Council when a vote on it is necessary.
   (G)   When a resolution is offered or a report or other matter is presented
to the Council, the presiding officer may accept it; if an objection is made,
then the presiding officer shall require a formal motion.
   (H)   When a motion has been made by a Council member, the presiding officer
shall state it, or if written, cause it to be read by the Clerk-Treasurer
before it is debated.
   (I)   After a motion made by a Council member has been stated by the
presiding officer or read by the Clerk-Treasurer, it may be withdrawn by the
Council member only if there has been no vote on, or amendment to, the motion.
   (J)   When a question is under debate before the Council, any motion to
adjourn, to lay on the table, or to call the previous question shall be in
order; these motions shall be voted on without a debate. Any member may move to
postpone to a day certain, to refer to, or amend, or to postpone indefinitely.
Except as otherwise provided, the motions shall have precedence in the order in
which they are arranged in this division (J).
   (K)   A motion to adjourn and a motion to fix a time at which the Council
shall adjourn shall always be in order. A motion to fix a time at which to
adjourn shall take precedence over a motion to adjourn.
   (L)   A motion to table an amendment shall not conclude debate on the motion
proposed to be amended.
   (M)   A motion before the Council calling for the previous question shall
have the effect of calling for a vote by the Council on the matter then under
discussion.
   (N)   A motion to postpone a matter before the Council to a day certain can
be amended only by altering the date of postponement.
   (O)   A motion to amend a motion before the Council and a motion to amend
that amendment shall always be in order. It shall also be in order to offer a
further amendment by way of a substitute, to which only one amendment may be
offered.
   (P)   A motion postponed indefinitely shall be taken up again only by
majority vote of the Council. No motion postponed indefinitely may be taken up
at the same or next succeeding meeting of the Council at which it was
postponed.
   (Q)   Every amendment shall be germane.
   (R)   When a motion has been carried or lost, any Council member voting with
the majority may move for the reconsideration of the vote at either at the same
or at the next succeeding meeting. A motion to reconsider shall take precedence
over all other motions except a motion to adjourn. Debate on a motion to
reconsider shall be in order only if the main question is debatable.
   (S)   Every ordinance shall be numbered in the order in which it is
introduced to the Council.
   (T)   Every ordinance shall be read three times in its entirety before voted
on by the Council. The ordinance may be read by title only at the second and
third reading if so ordered by majority vote of the Council.
   (U)   If the President of the Council is absent or otherwise unable to
preside, the Council shall designate a member to serve as presiding officer.
(Prior Code, § 30.03)
§ 35.13 SPECIAL MEETINGS.
   (A)   The presiding officer may call special meetings.
   (B)   Except as otherwise required by state law, notice shall be given for
special meetings according to the following procedure.
      (1)   Notice of the date, time, and place of the meeting shall be given
at least 48 hours before the meeting by posting a copy of the notice at the
door of Town Hall or by mailing written notices to each Council member and all
news media who have filed requests for such notices as set out in state law.
The Clerk-Treasurer shall retain copies of the notices given under this
division and shall file a verified statement that notice was properly given.
      (2)   The Council may conduct emergency meetings upon giving the notice
required by state law.
      (3)   Every Council meeting shall be open to the public. The Council may
not enter into executive session except in compliance with state law.
      (4)   The Town Hall shall be open for business from 9:00 a.m. to 5:00
p.m. on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday of each week. The Town
Hall shall be closed on Saturdays, Sundays, and all legal holidays.
(Prior Code, § 30.04)
§ 35.14 LEGAL HOLIDAYS DESIGNATED.
   (A)   The Council shall designate all town legal holidays in the annual
salary ordinance.
   (B)   This section shall not effect any action taken by the Council during
regular or special sessions. Any action taken by the Council on any legal
holiday shall be valid for all purposes.
(Prior Code, § 30.05)
Cross-reference:
   Paid holidays, see §
33.065
Statutory reference:
   Legal holidays, see I.C. 1-1-9-1
§ 35.15 ASSOCIATION MEMBERSHIP DUES.
   (A)   The Council is authorized to budget and appropriate funds to provide
for the payment of membership dues for the town, its elected and appointed
officials, and the members of its boards, councils, departments, or agencies in
any local, regional, state, or national associations of a civic, educational,
or governmental nature which have as their purpose the improvement of municipal
operations.
   (B)   The Council is authorized to budget and appropriate funds to pay the
expenses of duly authorized representatives to attend the meetings and
functions of organizations to which the town belongs.
   (C)   The Council shall designate the person or persons who shall represent
the town at any meeting or function of such organizations.
(Prior Code, § 30.06) (Ord. 125-83, passed 1-10-1983)
§ 35.16 COUNCIL TO SET COMPENSATION.
   (A)   The Council shall fix the compensation, salaries, and benefits of its
town officers and employees in a salary ordinance to be adopted annually.
   (B)   The Council may require that every town officer execute a bond payable
to the town in a sum approved by the Council and conditioned on the faithful
performance of the duties of the office and for the payment to the proper
person of monies received by the officer.
(Prior Code, § 30.07) (Ord. 122-82, passed 11-22-1982)
Statutory reference:
   Compensation of town officials, see I.C. 36-5-3-2
§ 35.17 ELECTRONIC COMMUNICATIONS.
   (A)   (1)   The resolution codified herein shall be known as the “Electronic
Meetings Policy” of the Council and applies to the Council and any committee
appointed directly by this Council or its presiding officer.   
      (2)   The provisions of I.C. 5-14-1.5-1 et seq., including definitions,
apply to the resolution codified herein.
   (B)   (1)   Subject to divisions (C) and (E) below, any member may
participate in a meeting by any electronic means of communication that:
         (a)   Allows all participating members of the governing body to
simultaneously communicate with each other; and
         (b)   Other than a meeting that is an executive session, allows the
public to simultaneously attend and observe the meeting.
      (2)   A member who participates by an electronic means of communication
shall be considered present for purposes of establishing a quorum and may
participate in final action only if the member can be seen and heard.
      (3)   All votes taken during a meeting at which at least one member
participates by an electronic means of communication must be taken by roll call
vote.
   (C)   (1)   At least 50% of the members must be physically present at a
meeting at which a member will participate by means of electronic
communication. Not more than 50% of the members may participate by an
electronic means of communication at that same meeting.
      (2)   A member may not attend more than a 50% of the meetings in a
calendar year by an electronic means of communication unless the member’s
electronic participation is due to:
         (a)   Military service;
         (b)   Illness or other medical condition;
         (c)   Death of a relative; or
         (d)   An emergency involving actual or threatened injury to persons or
property.
      (3)   A member may attend two consecutive meetings (a set of meetings) by
electronic communication. A member must attend in person at least one meeting
between sets of meetings that the member attends by electronic communication
unless the member’s absence is due to:
         (a)   Military service;
         (b)   Illness or other medical condition;
         (c)   Death of a relative; or
         (d)   An emergency involving actual or threatened injury to persons or
property.
   (D)   The minutes or memoranda of a meeting at which any member participates
by electronic means of communication must:
      (1)   Identify each member who:
         (a)   Was physically present at the meeting;
         (b)   Participated in the meeting by electronic means of
communication; and
         (c)   Was absent.
      (2)   Identify the electronic means of communication by which:
         (a)   Members participated in the meeting; and
         (b)   Members of the public attended and observed the meeting if the
meeting was not an executive session.
   (E)   No member of the Council may participate by means of electronic
communication in a meeting at which the Council may take final action to:
      (1)   Adopt a budget;
      (2)   Make a reduction in personnel;
      (3)   Initiate a referendum;
      (4)   Impose or increase a fee;
      (5)   Impose or increase a penalty;
      (6)   Exercise the Council’s power of eminent domain; or
      (7)   Establish, impose, raise, or renew a tax.
   (F)   If an emergency is declared by the governor under I.C. 10-14-3-12 or
the Council President under I.C. 10-14-3-29, members are not required to be
physically present for a meeting until the emergency is terminated.
   (G)   Members may participate in a meeting by any means of communication
provided that:
      (1)   At least a quorum of the members participate in the meeting by
means of electronic communication or in person;
      (2)   The public may simultaneously attend and observe the meeting unless
the meeting is an executive session; and
      (3)   The minutes or memoranda of the meeting must comply with division
(D) above.
   (H)   All votes taken during a meeting at which at least one member
participates by an electronic means of communication must be taken by roll call
vote.
(Res. 21-102, passed 6-14-2021)
§ 35.18 INDEPENDENT PURCHASING AGENCY.
   (A)   The Town Clerk Purchasing Agency (the “Purchasing Agency”) is
established as the purchasing agency for the town.
   (B)   The Purchasing Agency shall have all the powers and duties authorized
under I.C. 5-22 as may be supplemented from time to time by ordinances adopted
by the Council and policies adopted by the Purchasing Agency.
   (C)   The Purchasing Agency shall act as the purchasing agency for every
agency, board, office, branch, bureau, commission, council, department, or
other establishment of the governmental body of the town.
   (D)   The Purchasing Agency may designate in writing any employee of the
town as a purchasing agent.
(Ord. 101-98, passed 6-8-1998)
§ 35.19 TOWN CREDIT/DEBIT CARDS.
   (A)   The Council has authorized the Clerk-Treasurer to apply for a credit
line through PNC Bank in the amount of $18,000. Each issued card will have the
name of the employee in the above named position and will have a credit limit
of $2,000 per card.
   (B)   The Cities and Towns Bulletin and Uniform Compliance Guidelines (lune
2000) issued by the State Board of Accounts states in part that:
      (1)   Care should be taken that no interest, carrying charges, or
penalties are incurred due to late payments.
      (2)   All claims filed in connection with use of the credit cards must be
itemized as provided in I.C. 5-11-10 before being approved and paid. If the
claims are not itemized or properly documented, they shall be returned to the
employee user for correction.
      (3)   If any interest or penalty is incurred due to late filing or the
furnishing of documentation by an employee, the employee shall be held
responsible for paying the interest or penalty charges.
   (C)   In regards to sales tax, the employee needs to request a sales exempt
form prior to use from the Clerk-Treasurer for the removal of sales tax. If
sales tax is incurred, the employee shall be responsible for payment of the
tax.
   (D)   Each card holder, at the end of each year by December 31, shall show
physical proof of the card that it is in his or her possession to the Clerk-
Treasurer.
   (E)   The Council is desirous that this credit card not be used for purchase
of any personal items. Each user of the credit card will get permission from
the Clerk-Treasurer or Utility Clerk before each use.
      (1)   A sign out sheet will be made available, and each user will sign
out the card for each use.
      (2)   The sign out sheet will have the name of the user, time, date it is
being used, purpose of use, fund being charged, and the date/time the card is
returned to Clerk-Treasurer.
   (F)   Credit cards will not be kept overnight without the consent of Clerk-
Treasurer or Utility Clerk.
(Ord. 116-22, passed 10-10-2022)
§ 35.20 EMERGENCY ELECTRIC SERVICE.
   When electricians for the town are on vacation or sick leave, the town has
made arrangements for a qualified and licensed standby at a basic rate of $30.
If he or she is called out, he or she will be paid $20 per hour for each hour
worked. Two Council members shall specify emergency status.
(Prior Code, § 31.03) (Ord. 219-94, passed 8-8-1994; Ord. 227-1-95, passed - -
1995; Ord. 236-1-96, passed 8-19-1996)
§ 35.21 LICENSE REQUIRED FOR VEHICLE OPERATION.
   Any employee working for the town and operating a vehicle which would
require a commercial drivers license will be reimbursed by the town for the $28
testing fee and the $35 physical fee required to obtain a commercial driver’s
license.
(Prior Code, § 31.04) (Res. 92-132, passed 2-10-1992)
§ 35.22 EQUAL DISTRIBUTION OF OVERTIME.
   The hours of overtime standby each week shall be equally distributed among
the town employees so that no one or two employees are paid all the overtime.
(Prior Code, § 31.05) (Res. 88-103, passed 5-9-1988)
§ 35.23 INDOT AGREEMENTS.
   (A)   The President of the Council is hereby authorized and confirmed to
have the authority to execute any INDOT agreements and all other documents
related thereto and all documents related to any INDOT grant programs for and
on behalf of and to bind the town.
   (B)   The Council hereby ratifies all prior agreements and associated
documents executed by the President of the Council prior to the effective date
of the resolution codified herein.
(Res. 21-105, passed 11-8-2021)
§ 35.24 SMOKING.
   The Council approves the following smoking policy. The town shall take any
and all necessary action that may be required to implement such policy and to
review, monitor, and update as necessary the smoking policy.
   (A)   The town does not discriminate against any employee or employee group,
nor are any special rights afforded to either tobacco product users or non-
tobacco product users by this policy. The town does not and will not
discriminate against an applicant or employee based on his or her use of
tobacco products outside the course of employment.
   (B)   This policy should be implemented in a spirit of cooperation,
courtesy, and mutual respect. In addition, this policy is implemented to ensure
the town is complaint with applicable laws. The harassment of either a tobacco
product user employee or non-tobacco product user employee will not be
tolerated. Violations of this policy will be subject to discipline up to and
including termination.
   (C)   Tobacco product use is not permitted within any town facility or
within eight feet of any public entrance to a town facility. Signs or similar
notices stating “State Law Prohibits Smoking Within Eight Feet of This
Entrance” shall be posted and shall be maintained at all public entrances to
town facilities.
   (D)   This policy shall be implemented in the following manner:
      (1)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be posted at the town’s
offices where employment posters or other notices to employees are located.
      (2)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be provided to each of the
town’s employees and elected and appointed officials.
      (3)   A copy of the resolution codified herein in its entirety and the
attachments thereto, including this policy, shall be made available at the
office of the Clerk-Treasurer for public inspection.
      (4)   Signs stating “State Law Prohibits Smoking Within Eight Feet of
This Entrance” shall be posted and maintained at all public entrances to town
facilities.
      (5)   Any other such action(s) that would communicate the policies
established herein to the employees and elected or appointed officials of the
town may be taken to implement this policy.
      (6)   This policy is deemed implemented by the town upon the taking of
any of the above actions.
(Res. 88-96, passed - -; Res. 12-105, 6-11-2012)
§ 35.25 PUBLIC RECORDS; USE FOR COMMERCIAL PURPOSES PROHIBITED.
   (A)   Prohibited use. No individual or entity receiving public records under
I.C. 5-14-3-3(d) shall use such records for commercial purposes. This
prohibition does not apply to records used for news reporting, nonprofit
activities, or academic research.
   (B)   Discretion to deny. If the town reasonably believes that a requester
intends to use public records for commercial purposes, the town may deny the
request.
(Ord. 104-25, passed - -2025)

§ 50.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GARBAGE. Rejected food wastes, and includes every waste accumulation of
animal, fruit, or vegetable matter used or intended for food or that attends
the preparation, use, cooking, and dealing in or storing of meat, fish, fowl,
fruit, or vegetables.
   JUNK. Any worn out, cast-off, or discarded article or material prepared for
destruction; or collected or stored for salvage or conversion to some other
use; and any unwholesome or combustible substance or material which creates an
obnoxious condition and is injurious to health or offensive to the aesthetic
sense, including but not limited to any appliance incapable of being operated
under its own power and any fire hazard. Any article or material which,
unaltered or unchanged and without further conditioning, can be used for its
original purpose as readily as when new shall not be deemed JUNK.
   JUNKYARD. Any lot, parcel of real estate, or building used for storing,
salvaging, or processing garbage, junk, or rubbish or offering any such
material for sale. JUNKYARD does not mean any lot, parcel of real estate, or
building used by any foundry or manufacturing plant for the storage of garbage,
junk, or rubbish ordinarily in the operation of the foundry or manufacturing
business.
   PERSON. Any individual, firm, corporation, partnership, association, or
society.
   RUBBISH. Any waste material other than garbage or offal, including but not
limited to: wire, chips, shavings, bottles, broken glass, crockery, tin, cast
or wooden ware, hoses, rags, stumps, tree trunks, paper, boots, shoes, ashes,
metal, dirt, sweepings, boxes, cans, and wood.
(Prior Code, § 50.01) (Ord. 14, passed 2-24-1891; Ord. 29, passed - -1898; Ord.
200, passed 1-18-1960) Penalty, see §
50.99
Statutory reference:
   Littering, see I.C. 35-45-3-2
§ 50.02 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall collect garbage or rubbish without having first obtained a
license.
(Prior Code, § 50.02) (Ord. 242, passed 10-18-1965; Ord. 342, passed 5-12-1980)
Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.03 APPLICATION FOR LICENSE.
   Every applicant for a waste disposal license shall complete a written
application and shall file it in the office of the Clerk-Treasurer. The
application shall state:
   (A)   The name and present address of the applicant;
   (B)   The address of the applicant during the four years prior to the date
of the application;
   (C)   If the applicant has any employment other than the collection of
garbage and rubbish;
   (D)   If the applicant has a state chauffeur’s license and, if so, the
license number;
   (E)   The type of vehicle to be used, giving the year, model, and type of
bed or body and capacity of the vehicle; and
   (F)   A statement by the applicant indicating his or her intention of
complying with all laws, ordinances, rules, and regulations governing the
disposal of the garbage and rubbish.
(Prior Code, § 50.03) (Ord. 242, passed 10-18-1965; Ord. 342, passed 5-12-1980)
Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.04 LICENSE FEE; INSURANCE.
   (A)   (1)   Any person, firm, partnership, or corporation collecting rubbish
or garbage in the town shall submit to the office of the Clerk-Treasurer a
yearly license fee in the amount of $25 and proof of insurance of a combined
single limit of $1,000,000.
      (2)   The license will expire on December 31 each year and shall be
renewed on or before January 31.
   (B)   The applicant shall furnish a certificate or duplicate insurance
policy indicating that he or she carries insurance for property damage in an
amount not less than $25,000, liability insurance with limits of not less than
$100,000 for each person, and up to $300,000 for any one accident to the Clerk-
Treasurer before the issuance of the license.
(Prior Code, § 50.05)
(Ord. 242, passed 10-18-1965; Ord. 342, passed 5-12-1980; Ord. 102-06, passed
4-10-2006) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.05 REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   Any license issued under the terms of this section may be revoked by
the Council upon showing that the applicant is not collecting garbage and
rubbish in a satisfactory manner or upon his or her conviction for any
violation of state or town law.
   (B)   The Council shall conduct a hearing to determine if any license should
be revoked. The license holder shall receive written notice of the hearing at
least ten days before it occurs.
(Prior Code, § 50.06) (Ord. 242, passed 10-18-1965; Ord. 342, passed 5-12-1980)
Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.06 RULES OF COLLECTION.
   The licensee shall submit a copy of the schedule and policies governing his
or her collection of garbage and rubbish. The schedule and policies shall enter
into effect upon approval of the Council.
(Prior Code, § 50.07) (Ord. 242, passed 10-18-1965; Ord. 342, passed 5-12-1980)
Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.07 STORAGE OF JUNK.
   No person shall deposit, store, or maintain any garbage, junk, or rubbish
except within a building.
(Prior Code, § 50.08) (Ord. 14, passed 2-24-1891; Ord. 29, passed - -1898; Ord.
200, passed 1-18-1960) Penalty, see §
50.99
§ 50.08 ENFORCEMENT; NOTICE OF VIOLATION.
   (A)   Any violation of §
50.07 shall constitute a public nuisance.
   (B)   (1)   If any person in possession of any property within the town
permits a violation of §
50.07 to occur, the Council shall order the Police Department to post a written
notice on the property or to serve the notice personally or by certified mail
upon that person.
      (2)   If a firm or corporation is in possession of the property, the
notice may be served at its place of business or by certified mail. The notice
shall state that the violation must be corrected no later than seven days
following the receipt of the notice.
(Prior Code, § 50.09) (Ord. 14, passed 2-24-1891; Ord. 29, passed - -1898; Ord.
200, passed 1-18-1960)
§ 50.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   (1)   Any person violating any provision of §§
50.01 through
50.03 or §§ 5.05 through
50.06 shall be fined not less than $3 nor more than $2,500. Each day a
violation continues constitutes a separate offense.
      (2)   If any person fails to correct the violation before the expiration
of the period stated in the notice, that person shall be fined not less than $3
and not more than $10. Each day a violation continues constitutes a separate
offense.
(Prior Code, § 50.99)
   (B)   Failure to renew by the deadline in §
50.04(A) will result in a $10 per day late fee not to exceed $100.
Noncompliance of §
50.04 will result in a citation for ordinance violation if hauling continues
after failure to pay.
(Ord. 14, passed 2-24-1891; Ord. 29, passed - -1898; Ord. 200, passed 1-18-
1960; Ord. 102-06, passed 4-10-2006)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 51.01 CERTAIN ORGANIZATIONS EXEMPT FROM YEAR-ROUND PAYMENT.
   The Council will allow all tax-exempt, nonprofit organizations to be able to
be exempt from paying year-round utilities; for example, Frankton Heritage
Days, Frankton Pee Wee League, and the like; as their utility consumption is
often less than six months out of the year.
(Prior Code, § 51.01) (Res. 89-121, passed 2-13-1989)
§ 51.02 UNPAID BALANCE ON RENTED PROPERTY.
   The town will not accept payment for electric or water deposits from a new
tenant if a landlord or property owner owes an outstanding balance on his or
her utility account or a sewage balance on a previous tenant’s account of that
particular property.
(Prior Code, § 51.02) (Ord. 210-92, passed 10-12-1992)
§ 51.03 CREDIT HISTORY FEE.
   As of March 14, 1988, there will be a $2 fee for letters of a credit
history. This saves a former utility customer a large deposit when moving to
another town.
(Prior Code, § 51.03) (Res. 88-102, passed - -)
§ 51.04 METER READINGS.
   (A)   Utility customers are responsible for furnishing meter readings to the
town hall between the first to the tenth day of each month when meters are not
accessible to the meter reader for reasons such as dogs, fences, patio
enclosures, cars parked over meters, etc. These readings can be furnished by
phone, mail, fax, drop box, or in person.
   (B)   Any customer who does not furnish a meter reading to Town Hall will
receive an estimated bill each month. When a meter is estimated for a period of
four consecutive months, the meter will be disconnected on the tenth day of the
fourth month, at which time a meter reading and a $35 reconnect fee per utility
must be submitted to Town Hall before service will be restored.
   (C)   Adjustments will not be made to bills for over-estimated meters.
   (D)   A customer will owe a minimum bill on that particular meter each month
until usage reaches the estimated reading.
(Ord. 105-99, passed 9-13-1999; Ord. 108-21, passed 9-13-2021)
§ 51.05 DEPARTMENT OF STORM WATER MANAGEMENT.
   (A)   The Council adopts the provisions of I.C. 8-1.5-5 establishing a
Department of Storm Water Management governed by a Board of Storm Water
Management and a storm water district.
   (B)   The Council creates a special taxing district that includes all
territory within the town’s corporate boundaries as provided in I.C. 8-1.5-5-5.
   (C)   The Council establishes a Board of Storm Water Management which shall
consist of three directors (one member shall serve a one-year term or until his
or her successor is appointed; one member shall serve a two-year term or until
his or her successor is appointed; and one member shall serve a three-year term
or until his or her successor is appointed), each appointed by the President of
the Council, not more than two of whom may be of the same political party as
provided in I.C. 8-1.5-5-4.
(Ord. 115-21, passed 12-13-2021)
§ 51.06 JURISDICTION.
   The town’s electric and water utilities are hereby removed from the
jurisdiction of the State Utility Regulatory Commission.
(Ord. 101-01, passed 7-9-2001)
§ 51.07 RECONNECTION TIMES.
   A utility customer, when disconnected for nonpayment of utilities, must
contact Town Hall prior to 5:00 p.m. that day with normal payment procedures in
order to have service restored. If contact is made after 5:00 p.m., the
customer will remain without utilities until 7:00 a.m. minimum the next day.
(Ord. 103-03, passed 7-14-2003)
§ 51.08 SERVICE FEES; AFTER HOURS.
   A $25 service fee shall be charged to any customer for each occasion that a
utility employee is called out on overtime for a non-emergency situation.
Overtime is considered any time other than the hours of Monday through Friday
from 7:00 a.m. to 3:30 p.m., with the exception of designated holidays.
(Ord. 107-02, passed 10-14-2002)

PAYMENT PROCEDURES
§ 51.20 BILLING PROCEDURES; DUE DATE.
   (A)   The Council directs the town’s utility companies (electric, water, and
sewage) to prescribe to the following payment procedures in §§
51.20,
51.21(A),
51.22, and
51.23.
   (B)   Customers who pay utilities on a monthly basis must pay in full by the
fifth of each month. Those accounts not paid by the fifth of the month will be
charged late charges at the rate set by the State Utility Regulatory
Commission.
(Prior Code, § 51.15) (Ord. 172-87, passed - -; Ord. 174-88, passed - -; Ord.
179-88-A, passed - -)
§ 51.21 DELINQUENCY/DEFAULT; NOTICE; DISCONNECTION.
   (A)   Customers who have not paid their current utility bills by the next
billing will become delinquent and will be notified that the delinquent bill
must be paid within ten days unless an agreement is made with the utility
office. Normal procedure for that agreement is to pay current billing plus $50
to catch up the delinquent account. If the agreement is broken, service will be
disconnected without notice.
(Prior Code, § 51.16)
   (B)   Any utility customer who has a problem with paying his or her utility
bills may contact the utility company and sign an agreement. However, failure
to keep that agreement will place the customer in default and service will be
disconnected.
(Prior Code, § 51.19)
   (C)   The utility office will no longer issue a notice of delinquency to
customers after the fifth of the month as the “notice to disconnect” is now
printed on the back side of the regular monthly utility bill.
   (D)   Due to an increase of bankruptcies, foreclosures, and transients, the
current system allows a customer to have utilities for 90 days without making a
payment.
      (1)   In order not to cause hardship, customers currently owing more than
one month will receive a notice to pay their current bill plus $25 until the
bill is current. The delinquent bill will be due by the fifteenth of each
month.
      (2)   Disconnects will be done after the fifteenth of the month if no
payment is received, and a $35 reconnect fee per utility will be due in
addition to the amount of the utility bill.
      (3)   Arrangements to extend payment can be made; however, no extension
will be allowed past the fifth of the following month.
(Ord. 172-87, passed - -; Ord. 174-88, passed - -; Ord. 179-88-A, passed - -;
Ord. 103-02, passed 6-10-2002; Ord. 103-10, passed 5-10-2010; Ord. 108-21,
passed 9-13-2021)
§ 51.22 BUDGET CUSTOMERS.
   (A)   Budget customers who do not pay their budget agreement payment by the
fifth of each month will be penalized at the same rate as monthly customers. A
budget payment not paid by the next billing cycle will be delinquent, and
services will be disconnected without further notice. The normal procedure for
delinquent budget consumers is to pay current billing plus $50 to catch up
delinquent accounts.
   (B)   Budget customers must pay their outstanding balance no later than
September 1 of any given year. Those customers with credit balances will have
their balances credited to their accounts.
(Prior Code, § 51.17) (Ord. 172-87, passed - -; Ord. 174-88, passed - -; Ord.
179-88-A, passed - -)

   Project Safe customers will have 30 days to pay their accounts in full or
sign an agreement with the utility office. Failure to keep the agreement will
be cause for disconnection (after March 15).
(Prior Code, § 51.18) (Ord. 172-87, passed - -; Ord. 174-88, passed - -; Ord.
179-88-A, passed - -)
§ 51.24 REMITTANCE OF PAYMENT.
   The town will require a customer to remit entire payment of any arrearage
and current amount of his or her utility bill each month for the following
reasons:
   (A)   The customer has been disconnected three times in a 12-month period;
   (B)   The customer has filed a bankruptcy which included town utilities;
and/or
   (C)   The customer has moved back to the town and previously left the town
owing a utility bill which was turned over to the collection agency.
(Prior Code, § 51.20) (Ord. 100-97, passed 3-10-1997)

   (A)   Each new resident or business that wishes to install utilities will

52.23,

54.037. The only exception being that if the property is situated so that the
area around or near is undeveloped, the maximum electric, water, sewer, and
storm drain line size will be required as to aid in future development. All
cost will be borne by the owner or developer.
   (B)   Electric and water meter installation charges are increased to $25
each. An electric or water meter is billable monthly whether or not consumption
is used. This service is generally requested by having meters removed to avoid
minimum monthly utility charges for zero consumption when a resident will be
absent from his or her property by two or more consecutive months. The fee is
payable at such time meters are reinstalled.
(Ord. 101-03, passed 5-12-2003; Ord. 103-06, passed 4-10-2006)

refers to all payment extension beyond the fifteenth regular disconnect date.

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 52.01 CURTAILMENT RULES.
   The utility may curtail electric service according to the following
procedures. When the utility is notified that its supplier is implementing each
of the following steps, the utility will also implement these steps
immediately.
   (A)   Service Priority Class VIII customers shall be notified by press
release to fully curtail their service.
   (B)   After the public notice is given to Service Priority Class VIII
customers, curtailment of service to Service Priority Class VII customers will
begin and shall continue until a service level is reached of not more than 90%
percent but not less than 80% of service obligation or base monthly
consumption, whichever is applicable. If further curtailments are necessary,
curtailment will commence and proceed sequentially as necessary through Service
Priority Classes VI, V, IV, III and II customers, provided that the curtailment
to each succeeding lower numbered Service Priority Class shall be at least 10%
but not more than 20% percent less than the next higher-numbered Service
Priority Class beginning with Service Priority Class VII customers.
   (C)   When Service Priority Class VII is curtailed to a level of 35% of
service obligation or base monthly consumption, any further necessary
curtailments shall be made from next succeeding Service Priority Classes,
beginning with Service Priority Class VI, until each class through Service
Priority II reaches 35% level, provided that each reduction, until a Service
Priority Class shall be curtailed to the 35% level, shall maintain the interval
of at least 10% but not more than 20% between succeeding Service Priority
Classes.
   (D)   After Service Priority Classes VII, VI, V, IV, III, and II are
curtailed to a level of not more than 35% of service obligation or base monthly
consumption, Service Priority Classes VII through II inclusive will be further
curtailed by equal percentages until 100% curtailment occurs.
   (E)   After Service Priority Classes VII, VI, V, IV, III, and II are in 100%
curtailment, curtailment shall commence in Service Priority Class I as
necessary.
(Prior Code, § 52.01) (Ord. 325, passed 2-21-1978)
§ 52.02 PEAK MANAGEMENT CREDIT.
   (A)   IMPA has adopted a peak management program for the purpose of
implementing direct load control of central air conditioners and electric water
heaters; and, to encourage participation in the peak management program, IMPA
will provide a credit against a member’s purchased power expense for each
switch installed on its customers’ appliances in the amount of $1 per month
(year round) for water heaters and $2 per month (June through September only)
for central air conditioners.
   (B)   (1)   There shall be and there is established, for the use of and the
service rendered by the Town Utility, the peak management credit described in
the appendix attached to Ord. 224-95; and
      (2)   All ordinances and parts of ordinances in conflict herewith are
repealed; provided however, that the peak management credit described herein
shall not be in full force and effect unless and until the same shall be
approved by the State Utility Regulatory Commission, and until such time as the
approval of the Commission shall direct.
      (3)   This section shall be in full force and effect from and after its
passage; provided however, that the peak management credit described herein
shall not become effective unless approved by the State Utility Regulatory
Commission.
(Prior Code, § 52.02) (Ord. 224-95, passed 3-13-1995)

§ 52.06 INCENTIVES; CREDITS.
   The Council hereby adopts the local economic development rider, and such
adoption shall be effective immediately.
   (A)   This local economic development rider (“rider”) is available to
qualifying customers (as defined herein) to encourage new operations or
expansions of existing operations within the service territory of the town’s
electric utility (the “utility”).
   (B)   A QUALIFYING CUSTOMER is a new or existing non-residential customer in
the utility’s service territory that is establishing new operations or
expanding existing operations such that the new or expanded operations will
result in new or additional demand of at least one MW (1,000 kW) at one
delivery point (the “qualifying demand”), and the new or expanded operations
has involved a capital investment of at least $1,000,000 within the utility’s
service territory.
      (1)   For a qualifying customer that is expanding operations, qualifying
demand is measured from the average monthly peak demand for the 12 months
immediately preceding the effective date specified in the service application
(as defined herein). For a qualifying customer that is establishing new
operations, qualifying demand is measured from zero.
      (2)   A QUALIFYING CUSTOMER is not a customer:
         (a)    With “new” demand that results from a change in ownership of an
existing establishment without qualifying new load;
         (b)   Renewing service following interruptions such as equipment
failure, temporary plant shutdown, strike, economic conditions, or natural
disaster; or
         (c)   That has shifted its load from one operation or customer to
another within the utility’s service territory.
      (3)   The utility may determine exclusively, without recourse by any
customer, whether an event has occurred that would prevent a customer from
being a qualifying customer.
   (C)   Beginning with the effective date specified in a qualifying customer’s
service application, the utility will receive from Indiana Municipal Power
Agency (“IMPA”) a credit on the utility’s wholesale bill for the qualifying
demand of such qualifying customer.
      (1)   The utility will pass on the full amount of such credit to the
qualifying customer on each bill following the effective date specified in the
service application for a total of 60 months.
      (2)   For reference, the credit provided by IMPA and passed on to the
qualifying customer and applicable to the qualifying demand will be calculated
according to the following schedule:
 
Months 112  20%
Months 1324 15%
Months 2548 10%
Months 4960 5%
 
      (3)   In order to receive the credit as calculated above, the qualifying
customer must meet the minimum qualifying demand requirement during each month
of the incentive period (months one through 60). Failure to meet the minimum
qualifying demand requirement during a particular month will result in a credit
of zero for that particular month.
   (D)   The qualifying customer must submit a service application to the
utility specifying:
      (1)   A description of the amount and nature of the qualifying demand;
      (2)   The basis on which the qualifying customer meets the requirements
of this rider;
      (3)   The qualifying customer’s desired effective date; and
      (4)   Any other information required by the utility.
   (E)   This rider shall terminate upon the termination of IMPA’s
corresponding economic development rider, except that any qualifying customer
receiving a credit under this rider at the time of the rider’s termination may
continue to receive such credit for the remainder of the applicable incentive
period insofar as it continues to meet the requirements of this rider.
   (F)   This rider is applicable to the following rate schedules:
(Ord. 100-17, passed 6-12-2017)

feet of service line will be at the total cost of the owner.

and across said lots or subdivisions as in the town’s judgment will be properly

INTERCONNECTION STANDARDS
§ 52.40 APPLICATION.
   (A)   A customer desiring to interconnect renewable generation facilities
with the utility’s grid shall complete an interconnection application and
submit the application to the utility for review. After receipt of the
application, the utility shall conduct such further inspection of the renewable
generation facilities as the utility deems necessary and approve or deny the
application.
   (B)   If the application is denied, the utility shall provide a written
response to the customer explaining why the application was denied. The utility
is hereby authorized to charge a reasonable application fee to offset costs
involved with reviewing the application, inspecting the renewable generation
facilities, and otherwise ensuring compliance with this subchapter.
   (C)   If the interconnection application is approved, then the customer
agrees that no changes shall be made to the configuration of the renewable
generation facilities, as that configuration is described in the application,
and no relay or other control or protection settings specified in the
application shall be set, reset, adjusted, or tampered with except to the
extent necessary to verify that the renewable generation facilities comply with
the utility’s approved settings.
   (D)   It shall be unlawful for any person or entity to connect or maintain
the connection of a renewable generating facility to the utility’s grid without
obtaining the utility’s approval of an interconnection application.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.41 COMPLIANCE.
   In addition to such other requirements as the utility deems necessary, any
renewable generation facility allowed to interconnect to the utility’s grid
must comply with:
   (A)   The National Electrical Code and the National Electrical Safety Code,
as each may be revised from time to time;
   (B)   The utility’s rules and regulations and the utility’s general terms
and conditions for electric service, each as contained in the utility’s
electric tariff and each as may be revised from time to time; and
   (C)   All other applicable local, state, and federal codes and laws, as the
same may be in effect from time to time.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.42 CUSTOMER RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   For any approved renewable generation facilities interconnected to the
utility’s grid, the customer shall install, operate, and maintain, at the
customer’s sole cost and expense, the renewable generation facilities in
accordance with the Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers’
applicable Standard for Interconnecting Distributed Resources with Electric
Power Systems, as it may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   The customer shall be responsible for protecting, at the customer’s
sole cost and expense, the renewable generation facilities from any condition
or disturbance on the utility’s electric system including, but not limited to:
voltage sags or swells, system faults, outages, loss of a single phase of
supply, equipment failures, and lightning or switching surges.
   (C)   The customer shall operate any interconnected renewable generation
facilities in such a manner as not to cause undue fluctuations in voltage,
intermittent load characteristics, or otherwise interfere with the operation of
the utility’s electric system.
   (D)   At all times when the renewable generation facilities are being
operated in parallel with the utility’s electric system, the customer shall
operate the renewable generation facilities in a manner that no disturbance
will be produced to the service rendered by the utility to any of its other
customers or to any electric system interconnected with the utility’s electric
system.
   (E)   The customer’s control equipment for the renewable generation
facilities shall immediately, completely, and automatically disconnect and
isolate the renewable generation facilities from the utility’s electric system
in the event of a fault on the utility’s electric system, a fault on the
customer’
      (1)   The automatic disconnecting device included in such control
equipment shall not be capable of reclosing until after service is restored on
the utility’s electric system.
      (2)   Additionally, if the fault is with the customer’s renewable
generation facilities, such automatic disconnecting device shall not be
reclosed until after the fault is isolated from the customer’s renewable
generation facilities.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.43 INSPECTIONS; EMPLOYEE ACCESS.
   (A)   Upon reasonable advance notice to the customer, the utility shall have
access to any interconnected renewable generation facilities to perform on-site
inspections to:
      (1)   Verify that the installation and operation of the renewable
generation facilities comply with the requirements of this subchapter; and
      (2)   Verify the proper installation and continuing safe operation of the
renewable generation facilities.
   (B)   The utility shall also have at all times immediate access to breakers
or any other equipment that will isolate the renewable generation facilities
from the utility’s electric system.
   (C)   The utility shall not be responsible for any costs the customer may
incur as a result of such inspection(s).
   (D)   The utility shall have the right and authority to isolate approved
interconnected renewable generation facilities at the utility’s sole discretion
if the utility believes that:
      (1)   Continued interconnection and parallel operation of the renewable
generation facilities with the utility’s electric system creates or contributes
(or will create or contribute) to a system emergency on either the utility’s or
the customer’s electric facilities;
      (2)   The renewable generation facilities are not in compliance with the
requirements of this subchapter; or
      (3)   The renewable generation facilities interfere with the operation of
the utility’s electric system. In non-emergency situations, the utility shall
give the customer reasonable notice prior to isolating the renewable generation
facilities.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.44 INSURANCE; LIABILITY.
   (A)   The customer shall procure and keep in force, during all periods of
parallel operation of the renewable generation facilities with the utility’s
electric system, homeowners, commercial, or other insurance to protect the
interests of the utility as a named insured, with insurance carriers acceptable
to the utility, and in amounts not less than those reasonably determined by the
utility to be necessary taking into consideration the nameplate capacity,
configuration, and type of the renewable generation facilities.
   (B)   The customer shall indemnify and hold harmless the utility, the town,
its employees, representatives, agents, and subcontractors from and against all
claims, liability, damages and expenses, including attorney’s fees, based on
any injury to any person, including the loss of life, or damage to any property
including the loss of use thereof, arising out of, resulting from, or connected
with, or that may be alleged to have arisen out of, resulted from, or connected
with, an act or omission by the customer, its employees, agents,
representatives, successors or assigns in the construction, ownership,
operation or maintenance, of the customer’s renewable generation facilities.
   (C)   If the utility is required to bring an action to enforce its rights
under this section, either as a separate action or in connection with another
action, and said rights are upheld, the customer shall reimburse the utility
for all expenses, including attorney’s fees, incurred in connection with such
action.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018) Penalty, see §
52.99
§ 52.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person or entity found to be in violation of §
52.40(D) shall be fined not less than $500 nor more than $2,500 for each such
violation, plus costs. In addition to the foregoing fines and at the utility’s
sole discretion, property where a renewable generating facility is unlawfully
connected to the utility’s grid may be disconnected from the utility’s grid
until an interconnection application is approved. Every day that a violation of
§
52.40(D) occurs shall constitute a separate offense.
(Ord. 101-18, passed 7-9-2018)
