GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 70.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this traffic code, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ABANDONED VEHICLE. As follows:
      (1)   A vehicle located on public property illegally;
      (2)   A vehicle left on public property continuously without being moved
for three days;
      (3)   A vehicle located on public property in such a manner as to
constitute a hazard or obstruction to the movement of pedestrian or vehicle
traffic on a public right-of-way;
      (4)   A vehicle that has remained on private property without the consent
of the owner or person in control of that property for more than 48 hours;
      (5)   A vehicle from which there has been removed the engine,
transmission, or differential; or that is otherwise partially dismantled or
inoperable and left on public property;
      (6)   A vehicle that has been removed by a towing service or a public
agency upon request of an officer enforcing a statute or ordinance other than
this traffic code, if the vehicle once impounded is not claimed or redeemed by
the owner or his or her agent within 15 days of its removal; or
      (7)   A vehicle that is six or more model-years old and mechanically
inoperable and left on private property continuously in a location visible from
public property for more than 30 days.
   ALLEY. A street intended to provide access to the rear or side of lots or
buildings in urban districts and not intended for the use of through vehicular
traffic.
   AUTHORIZED EMERGENCY VEHICLE. As follows:
      (1)   Vehicles of the Fire Department, police vehicles, and such
ambulances as are operated by or for hospitals or health and hospital
corporations pursuant to I.C. 16-31-1 et seq.;
      (2)   Vehicles other than ambulances which are owned by persons, firms,
or corporations other than hospitals, are used in emergency service, and are
designated as emergency vehicles by the state’s Department of Highways; and
      (3)   Ambulances which are owned by persons, firms, or corporations other
than hospitals and which are approved by the state’s Emergency Medical Services
Commission.
   AUTOMOBILE SCRAPYARD. A business organized for the purpose of scrap metal
processing, automobile wrecking, or operating a junkyard.
   BICYCLE. Any foot-propelled vehicle, irrespective of the number of wheels in
contact with the ground.
   BUREAU. The state’s Bureau of Motor Vehicles acting directly or through its
duly authorized officers and agents.
   BUSINESS DISTRICT. The territory contiguous to and including a highway when
50% or more of the frontage thereon for a distance of 500 feet or more is
occupied by buildings in use for business.
   COMMISSIONER. The Commissioner of the Bureau.
   CROSSWALK.
      (1)   The part of a roadway at an intersection included within the
connections of the lateral lines of the sidewalks on opposite sides of the
highway measured from the curbs or, in the absence of curbs, from the edges of
the traversable roadway; and
      (2)   Any portion of a roadway at an intersection or elsewhere distinctly
indicated for pedestrian crossing by lines or other markings on the surface.
   DRIVER. Every person who drives or is in actual physical control of a
vehicle.
   HOLIDAY. Any day designated by the Council under §
35.14 of this code.
   INTERSECTION.
      (1)   The area embraced within the prolongation or connection of the
lateral curb lines or, if none, then the lateral boundary lines of the roadways
of two highways which join one another at, or approximately at, right angles;
or the area within which vehicles traveling upon different highways joining at
any other angle may come in conflict.
      (2)   Where a highway includes two roadways 30 feet or more apart, then
every crossing of each roadway of such divided highway by an intersection
highway shall be regarded as a separate INTERSECTION. In the event such
intersecting highway also includes two roadways 30 feet or more apart, then
every crossing of two roadways of such highway shall be regarded as a separate
INTERSECTION.
   LOADING ZONE. A space reserved for the exclusive use of vehicles during the
loading or unloading of passengers or property.
   OFFICER. As follows:
      (1)   A regular member of the State Police;
      (2)   A regular member of the Town Police Department;
      (3)   The Town Marshal or any Town Marshal Deputy;
      (4)   A regular member of the county’s police force; or
      (5)   Any individual of an agency designated to enforce this title.
   OFFICIAL TRAFFIC-CONTROL DEVICE. All signs, signals, markings, and devices
(including railroad advance warning signs) not inconsistent with this chapter,
placed, or erected by authority of a public body or official having
jurisdiction, for the purpose of regulating, warning, or guiding traffic.
   OFFICIAL TRAFFIC-CONTROL SIGNAL. Any device not inconsistent with this title
whether manually, electrically, or mechanically operated, by which traffic is
alternately directed to stop and to proceed.
   OWNER. A person who holds the legal title of a vehicle, or in the event a
vehicle is the subject of an agreement for the conditional sale or lease
thereof with the right of purchase upon performance of the conditions stated in
the agreement and with an immediate right of possession vested in the
conditional vendee or lessee. In the event a mortgagor of a vehicle is entitled
to possession, then such conditional vendee or lessee or mortgagor shall be
deemed the OWNER for the purpose of this act.
   PARKING. To stop or stand a vehicle except for a short period of time to
load or unload merchandise or passengers.
   PARKING SPACE. A designated portion of the surface of a highway of
sufficient length and depth to accommodate a vehicle of reasonable dimensions.
The designated portion shall be indicated and marked off as required by state
law.
   PARTS. All components of a vehicle which, as assembled, do not constitute a
complete vehicle.
   PEDESTRIAN. Any person afoot.
   PERSON. Every natural person, firm, co-partnership, association, or
corporation.
   PRIVATE PROPERTY. All property other than public property.
   PUBLIC AGENCY. The Police Department. The Police Department is given the
responsibility for removal, storage, and disposal of abandoned vehicles.
   PUBLIC PROPERTY. A public right-of-way, street, highway, alley, park, or
other state, county, or municipal property.
   RESIDENTIAL DISTRICT. The territory contiguous to and including a highway
not comprising a business district, when the property on such highway for a
distance of 500 feet or more is, in the main, improved with residences or
residences and buildings in use for business.
   RIGHT-OF-WAY. The privilege of the immediate use of the highway.
   ROADWAY. The portion of a highway improved, designed, or ordinarily used for
vehicular travel.
   SCRAP METAL PROCESSOR. An establishment having facilities for processing
iron, steel, or nonferrous scrap and whose principal product is scrap iron and
scrap steel or nonferrous scrap for sale for remelting purposes.
   SIDEWALK. The portion of a highway between the curb lines or the lateral
lines of a roadway and the adjacent property line intended for use by
pedestrians.
   STAND or STANDING. To halt a vehicle except for a short period of time to
receive or discharge merchandise or passengers.
   STOP or STOPPING.
      (1)   When required, means complete cessation of movement.
      (2)   When prohibited, means any halting (even momentarily) of a vehicle,
except when necessary to avoid conflict with other traffic or in compliance
with the directions of an officer or traffic-control device or signal.
   STREET or HIGHWAY. The entire width between boundary lines of every way
publicly maintained, when any part thereof is open to the use of the public for
purposes of vehicular travel.
   TOWING SERVICE. A business that engages in moving or removing disabled
vehicles and, once removed, to store or impound vehicles.
   TRAFFIC. Pedestrians, ridden or herded animals, street cars, vehicles, and
other conveyances, (either singly or together) while using any highway for
purposes of travel.
   TRUCK. Every motor vehicle designed, used, or maintained primarily for the
transportation of property.
   VEHICLE. Every device in, upon, or by which any person or property is or may
be transported or drawn upon a highway, except devices moved by human power or
used exclusively upon stationary rails or tracks.
(Prior Code, § 70.01)
Statutory reference:
   State law definitions, see I.C. 9-13-2
§ 70.02 TRAFFIC-CONTROL DEVICES.
   (A)   The Town Marshal may temporarily erect official traffic-control
devices when required by an emergency. The Town Marshal shall advise the
Council of this action at its next regular meeting.
(Prior Code, § 70.20)
   (B)   (1)   Crosswalks and safety zones. The Town Marshal may designate and
maintain, by appropriate devices, marks, or lines upon the surface of the
roadway, crosswalks at intersections where, in the opinion of the Council,
there is particular danger to pedestrians crossing the roadway and at other
places as it may deem necessary.
      (2)   Traffic lanes. The Town Marshal may mark traffic lanes upon the
roadway of any street or highway.
(Prior Code, § 70.21)
(Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT
§ 70.15 DUTY OF TOWN MARSHAL.
   The Town Marshal and Police Department shall enforce this title and I.C. 9-
13-1-1 et seq., make arrests for traffic violations, investigate accidents, and
cooperate with town officials in the administration of traffic laws and in
developing methods to improve traffic conditions.
(Prior Code, § 70.35) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.16 RECORDS OF TRAFFIC VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   The Police Department shall keep a record of all violations of this
title and I.C. 9-13-1-1 et seq., of which any person has been charged, together
with a record of the final disposition of all the alleged offenses. The record
shall indicate all types of violations and the total of each. The records shall
be maintained for at least five years from the date of the alleged violation.
   (B)   All forms for records and notices of violations shall be serially
numbered. Each record and notice shall be available for public inspection. A
written record shall be available for public inspection showing the disposal of
all the forms for each month and year.
(Prior Code, § 70.36) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.17 POLICE TO INVESTIGATE ACCIDENTS; STUDIES.
   (A)   The Town Marshal shall investigate traffic accidents and arrest and
assist in the prosecution of persons charged with violations of this title or
I.C. 9-13-1-1 et seq.
   (B)   The Police Department shall conduct studies of all locations where
accidents are numerous and recommend remedial measures to the Council.
(Prior Code, § 70.37) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.18 ACCIDENT REPORTS.
   (A)   The driver of any vehicle involved in an accident shall file a report
of the accident or a copy of any report required to be filed with the State
Police Department with the Town Police Department in accordance with I.C. 9-26-
1-7, as amended.
   (B)   Whenever the driver is physically incapable of making a written report
of the accident, and there was another occupant in the vehicle at the time of
the accident capable of making the report, that occupant shall file the report.
If there was no other occupant and if the driver is not the owner of the
vehicle, then the owner of the vehicle involved in the accident shall, within
five days after the accident, make the report not made by the driver.
   (C)   The Town Police Department shall maintain a suitable system of filing
traffic accident reports. Accident reports or cards referring to them shall be
filed alphabetically by location. The reports shall be for the confidential use
of the Police Department, except for the uses permitted under I.C. 9-26-2-3 and
I.C. 9-26-3-4.
(Prior Code, § 70.38) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.19 ANNUAL TRAFFIC SAFETY REPORT.
   (A)   The Police Department shall file an annual traffic safety report with
the Council.
   (B)   The report shall indicate:
      (1)   The number of traffic accidents;
      (2)   The number of persons killed;
      (3)   The number of persons injured;
      (4)   The number of traffic accidents investigated;
      (5)   Any recommendations for remedial measures;
      (6)   Any plans for future traffic safety activities; and
      (7)   Any other pertinent information.
(Prior Code, § 71.39) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.20 TRAFFIC ENGINEER.
   The Town Marshal shall serve as the Town Traffic Engineer.
(Prior Code, § 70.40) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this traffic code for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person who violates any portion of the traffic schedules provided
in
Chapter 74 (as amended from time to time) or the parking schedules provided in
Chapter 75 (as amended from time to time), whether or not the same constitute
moving violations, shall be fined in accordance with the code and the following
schedule, in addition to court costs and/or ordinance violation costs
prescribed under I.C. 33-37-4-2, which are established by the Indiana
Legislature and are subject to change:
Violations as Provided in Chapter 74 and Chapter 75
Violations as Provided in Chapter 74 and Chapter 75
1-5 mph over the speed limit                  $16.50
6-10 mph over the speed limit                 $26.50
11-15 mph over the speed limit                $41.50
16-20 mph over the speed limit                $61.50
21-25 mph over the speed limit                $81.50
26 mph over the speed limit (or greater)*     $150
Disregarding stop sign                        $36.50
Operating a snowmobile on a prohibited street $36.50
Parking                                       $36.50
Notes to Table:
*Must see judge
 
   (C)   The general and specific penalty sections of the code shall remain in
effect, except where a specific penalty set forth herein conflicts with the
specific or general penalty sections of the code, in which case the penalty set
forth above amends such conflicting specific or general penalty section of the
code.
   (D)   The town hereby authorizes the execution of an interlocal cooperation
agreement with the Town of Edgewood for the use of the Edgewood Traffic
Violations Bureau and Edgewood Town Court to hear and dispose of the violations
of the
Chapter 74 (Traffic Schedules) and
Chapter 75 (Parking Schedules) of the code to the extent allowed by law.
      (1)   The Edgewood Town Judge shall hear such traffic cases, which
constitute ordinance violations of the ordinances codified in this traffic
code, in all instances allowed by law in accordance with I.C. 34-28-5 et seq.,
and payment of fines may be accepted by the Clerk of the Edgewood Traffic
Violations Bureau in satisfaction thereof.
         (a)   Any person charged with an offense for which payment of a fine
may be made to the Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau shall have the option of
paying the fine within the time specified in the ordinance violation notice at
the Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau on entering an admission and on waiving
appearance in court; or may have the option of a trial as authorized by law.
         (b)   The payment of a fine to the Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau
shall be deemed an acknowledgment of the alleged violation.
         (c)   A person who enters into, and pays the fee for, a deferred
prosecution shall not be deemed to have admitted commission of the violation
for which the deferral was extended.
      (2)   The following duties are imposed on the Edgewood Traffic Violations
Bureau in reference to traffic offenses:
         (a)   It shall accept designated fines, issue receipts, and represent
in court those violators as are permitted and desire to admit violations and
waive court appearances.
         (b)   It shall ascertain which persons wish to be heard in court and
notify the arresting officer and witnesses, if any, to be present.
         (c)   It shall keep an easily accessible record of all violations of
which each person has been guilty during the preceding 12 months, whether the
guilt was established in court or in the Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau.
      (3)   The Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau shall follow the procedure
as may be prescribed by the traffic ordinances of the Town of Frankton or as
may be required by any laws of the state.
      (4)   The Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau shall determine, in
conjunction with the attorney for the Town of Frankton, those persons to whom
deferral shall be offered and shall notify such persons of their eligibility
and the procedures for entering into a deferral contract. It shall also receive
deferred prosecution payments, provide receipts to those making payments, and
account for the funds in compliance with rules established by the Clerk-
Treasurer of Frankton and state law.
      (5)   The Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau shall keep records and
submit summarized monthly reports to the judge for the Town of Edgewood and the
Clerk-Treasurer for the Town of Frankton of all notices issued and arrests made
for violations of the traffic laws and ordinances in the Town of Frankton and
of all the fines collected by the Edgewood Traffic Violations Bureau or the
Court for violations of the Town of Frankton’s code, and the status of every
case of violation of the provisions of the laws and ordinances. The records
shall be so maintained as to show all types of violations and the total of
each. The records shall be public records.
      (6)   There is hereby created a non-reverting fund for the purpose of
receiving fines imposed for town traffic ordinance violations issued pursuant
to the ordinance codified herein and
Chapters 74 and
75 of the code.
         (a)   The non-reverting fund hereby created shall be designated as the
Traffic Ordinance Violations Fund.
         (b)   Expenditures from the Traffic Ordinance Violations Fund shall be
limited to expenses for the Town of Frankton Police Department, Edgewood
Traffic Violations Bureau and Edgewood Town Court, the Town of Frankton
Ordinance Violations Bureau, the Town of Frankton legal expenses, and other
expenses required or allowed by state law.
   (E)   Except for offenses or violations under I.C. 9-24-6 involving the
operation of commercial motor vehicles, the town hereby establishes a deferral
program for deferring actions in accordance with I.C. 34-28-5-1, as follows:
      (1)   Actions may be deferred pursuant to this section if:
         (a)   The defendant in the action agrees to the conditions of a
deferral program offered by the attorney for the town;
         (b)   The defendant in the action agrees to pay the Clerk of the
Edgewood Town Court an initial user’s fee of $52 and a monthly user’s fee of
$10 for each month the person remains in the deferral program in addition to
penalty costs;
         (c)   The terms of the agreement are recorded in an instrument signed
by the defendant and the attorney for the Town of Frankton;
         (d)   The defendant agrees to pay a fee of $70 to the Clerk of the
Edgewood Town Court if the action involves a moving traffic offense as
described in I.C. 9-13-2-110; and
         (e)   The agreement is filed with the Edgewood Town Court.
      (2)   When a defendant complies with the terms of the agreement filed
with the Edgewood Town Court pursuant to this section, the attorney for the
Town of Frankton shall request the Court dismiss the action, and upon receipt
of such a request for dismissal of an action, the Court shall dismiss the
action. An action so dismissed may not be refiled.
(Ord. 101-15, passed 2-9-2015; Ord. 104-15, passed 6-8-2015)
CHAPTER 71: TRAFFIC REGULATIONS
Section
General Provisions
   
71.01   Police and Fire Departments to regulate traffic in emergency
   
71.02   Non-motorized traffic
   
71.03   Use of coasters, roller skates, and similar devices
   
71.04   Public employees to obey traffic rules
   
71.05   Authorized emergency vehicles
Loads and Equipment

71.21   Road weight limits

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 71.01 POLICE AND FIRE DEPARTMENTS TO REGULATE TRAFFIC IN EMERGENCY.
   Fire Department officers and members of the Police Department may direct
traffic in the immediate vicinity of a fire as conditions may require,
notwithstanding any provision in this code.
(Prior Code, § 71.01) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 71.02 NON-MOTORIZED TRAFFIC.
   Every person propelling a pushcart, riding an animal, or driving any animal
drawing a vehicle upon a roadway shall be subject to the provisions of this
chapter applicable to the driver of a vehicle except those provisions of this
chapter which by their very nature can have no application.
(Prior Code, § 71.02) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 71.03 USE OF COASTERS, ROLLER SKATES, AND SIMILAR DEVICES.
   No person upon roller skates or riding in or by means of any coaster, toy
vehicle, or similar device shall go upon any roadway except while crossing a
street on a crosswalk. When so crossing, the person shall be granted all the
rights and be subject to all of the duties applicable to pedestrians.
(Prior Code, § 71.03) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 71.04 PUBLIC EMPLOYEES TO OBEY TRAFFIC RULES.
   The provisions of this chapter shall apply to the drivers of all vehicles
owned or operated by the United States, this state, or any political
subdivision of the state, subject to such specific exceptions as are set forth
in this chapter or in I.C. 9-13-1-1 et seq. The provisions of this chapter
shall not apply to persons, motor vehicles, and other equipment while actually
engaged in work in a street but shall apply to such persons and vehicles when
traveling to or from work.
(Prior Code, § 71.04) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 71.05 AUTHORIZED EMERGENCY VEHICLES.
   (A)   The driver of any authorized emergency vehicle, when responding to an
emergency call or when in the pursuit of an actual or suspected violator of the
law or when responding to, but not upon returning from, a fire alarm may
perform any activity authorized under I.C. 9-21-1-8(b).
   (B)   The foregoing provision shall not relieve the driver of an authorized
emergency vehicle from the duty to drive with due regard for the safety of all
persons, nor shall such provision protect the driver from the consequences of
his or her reckless disregard for the safety of others.
(Prior Code, § 71.05) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
LOADS AND EQUIPMENT
§ 71.20 CONTAINMENT OF LOADS.
   No vehicle operator shall permit any material being transported by that
vehicle to fall, leak, or be discharged onto any highway or onto any property
abutting any highway. The operator shall remove any material which falls from
the vehicle and shall notify the Town Marshal immediately if any danger to the
public exists due to the discharge of this material. The operator shall be
liable for any damage caused by this discharge.
(Prior Code, § 71.20) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 71.21 ROAD WEIGHT LIMITS.
   (A)   No truck or other commercial vehicle with a gross weight of 10,000
pounds or more shall be permitted on any town highway, street, or alley except:
      (1)   Local delivery trucks; or
      (2)   Owners of trucks or commercial vehicles who live within the town
limits, and whose business is outside the town limits, and then only for
ingress or egress.
   (B)   The Town Street Department shall post signs giving notice of this
section on all access streets and roads to designate routes for trucks or
commercial vehicles with a gross weight of over 10,000 pounds.
(Prior Code, § 71.21) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981; Ord. 122-82, passed 11-
22-1982) Penalty, see §
70.99
Cross-reference:
   Designated truck routes, see
Ch. 74, Sch. IV
Statutory reference:
   Size and weight restrictions, see I.C. 9-20-3 and 9-20-4
PROCESSIONS AND ASSEMBLAGES
§ 71.35 PARADES AND THE LIKE; PERMIT.
   (A)   The Council, in accordance with I.C. 9-21-1-3(a)(3), regulates
processions or assemblages on the highways. No parade, procession, or
assemblage shall take place on any highway until the Town Marshal has issued a
permit for the event.
   (B)   The Town Marshal may require the applicant for a permit to observe
restrictions for public safety as a condition for the exercise of the permit,
but has no discretion to deny any application for a permit.
   (C)   This section does not apply to any funeral procession or parade
composed of members of the U.S. Armed Forces, state military forces, Police
Department, or the Fire Department.
(Prior Code, § 71.22) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
70.99
§ 71.36 FUNERAL PROCESSIONS IDENTIFIED.
   The Town Marshal shall approve the types of pennants, flags, windshield
stickers, or other identifying insignias to be displayed upon, or other methods
to be employed to identify, vehicles in funeral processions.
(Prior Code, § 71.23) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
70.99
CHAPTER 72: PARKING REGULATIONS
Section
   
72.01   Parking regulations adopted
   
72.02   Traffic obstruction prohibited
   
72.03   Alleys
   
72.04   Parking for vehicle sale or repairs prohibited
   
72.05   Obedience to parking signs or markings
   
72.06   Truck and bus parking
   
72.07   Parking off roadway
   
72.08   Parking in business district
   
72.09   Loading zones
 
   
72.99   Penalty
§ 72.01 PARKING REGULATIONS ADOPTED.
   The Council, in accordance with I.C. 9-21-1-3(a)(1), adopts the following
regulations governing the standing or parking of vehicles.
(Prior Code, § 72.01) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981)
§ 72.02 TRAFFIC OBSTRUCTION PROHIBITED.
   (A)   No person shall park any vehicle upon a highway so that less than ten
feet of the width of the roadway is available for the free movement of
vehicular traffic.
(Prior Code, § 72.02)
   (B)   (1)   Nothing is to be placed in a town street or alley which may even
temporarily impeded traffic at any time through any street or alley. Parking
shall be permitted along streets in designated marked spaces. Temporary parking
for the purpose of making deliveries, loading, or unloading shall be permitted
along streets to the extent it does not impeded traffic or cause a safety
hazard.
      (2)   Parking shall be prohibited upon any unimproved public right-of-way
owned by the town and upon all easements owned by the town except as expressly
permitted by the ordinance codified herein, a written agreement with the town,
or as required for town or utility operations.
(Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981; Ord. 192-89, passed 9-11-1989; Ord. 108-17,
passed 11-13-2017) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.03 ALLEYS.
   (A)   No person shall stop, stand, or park a vehicle in any alley longer
than necessary for the loading or unloading of passengers or merchandise.
   (B)   No person shall stand or park any vehicle in any alley so that less
than eight feet of width is available for the free movement of traffic. If less
than eight feet of width is available, the operator of the parked or standing
vehicle shall move it immediately upon the entrance of another vehicle into the
alley.
(Prior Code, § 72.03) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.04 PARKING FOR VEHICLE SALE OR REPAIRS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall park a vehicle upon any roadway for the principal purpose of
displaying the vehicle for sale. No person shall park a vehicle upon any
roadway for the principal purpose of repairing the vehicle except to the extent
required to make emergency repairs.
(Prior Code, § 72.04) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.05 OBEDIENCE TO PARKING SIGNS OR MARKINGS.
   No person shall park or stand a vehicle in any location which has been
officially signed or marked for parking on a highway or in a municipal parking
lot except within any markings and at the orientation indicated by the signs or
markings.
(Prior Code, § 72.05) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.06 TRUCK AND BUS PARKING.
   (A)   Residential district. No owner or operator of any truck, bus, trailer,
semitrailer, pole trailer, as defined by state law; or recreational vehicle,
tractor, or trailer, as defined by state law; which is taller, wider, or longer
than a conventional station wagon or pickup truck shall park that vehicle upon
any highway or alley within any residential district. These vehicles may park
for the time required to make deliveries or pick up merchandise if that parking
does not interrupt the regular traffic flow and does not create a safety
hazard.
   (B)   Business district. No vehicle listed in division (A) above shall park
upon any highway or alley within any business district longer than one hour.
These vehicles may park for the time required to make deliveries or pick up
merchandise in the regular course of business if that parking does not
interrupt the regular traffic flow and does not create a safety hazard.
(Prior Code, § 72.06) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.07 PARKING OFF ROADWAY.
   No person shall stand or park a vehicle on a highway other than on the
roadway or paved shoulder.
(Prior Code, § 72.07) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.08 PARKING IN BUSINESS DISTRICT.
   No person shall park a vehicle for more than 12 consecutive hours in any
business district.
(Prior Code, § 72.08) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
§ 72.09 LOADING ZONES.
   (A)   No person shall stop, stand, or park a vehicle in any place marked as
a loading zone for any purpose other than for the expeditious loading or
unloading of passengers or materials during the times when the regulations
applicable to that curb loading zone are effective.
   (B)   No person shall stop, stand, or park a vehicle during those times in
division (A) above for more than three minutes to load or unload passengers or
for more than 20 minutes to load or unload materials.
(Prior Code, § 72.09) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
72.99
Cross-reference:
   Loading zones designated, see
Ch. 75, Sch. II
§ 72.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
70.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Failure to comply with §
72.02(B) will result in the following actions.
      (1)   The town will issue a notification in writing to move the vehicle
within 24 hours.
      (2)   The town will implement at $25 fine to the vehicle owner if the
vehicle is not moved within 24 hours of notification.
      (3)   The town will tow, or cause to be towed, the vehicle at the owner’s
expense if it is not moved within 48 hours of the notification.
      (4)    In the event a vehicle is impeding access by a utility vehicle or
emergency vehicle, the vehicle must be moved immediately upon written or verbal
notice or it will be towed at the owner’s expense.
(Ord. 108-17, passed 11-13-2017)

BICYCLES
§ 73.01 BICYCLE REGULATION.
   (A)   The Council, in accordance with I.C. 9-21-1-3(a)(9), regulates the
operation of bicycles and requires their registration and licensing. These
regulations shall apply to the operation of any bicycle on any highway or path
reserved for the exclusive use of bicycles.
   (B)   No person having custody of or responsibility for any child shall
permit that child to violate any provision of this subchapter.
(Prior Code, § 73.01) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.02 BICYCLE DEALERS.
   Every person engaged in the business of buying or selling new or secondhand
bicycles shall maintain a record of every bicycle purchased or sold, giving the
name and address of the person from whom purchased or to whom sold, a
description of such bicycle by name or make, and the frame number of the
bicycle.
(Prior Code, § 73.02) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.03 TRAFFIC REGULATIONS APPLICABLE TO BICYCLE OPERATION.
   Every person operating a bicycle upon a roadway shall have all the rights
and all the duties under the provisions of this subchapter and I.C. 9-21-11
applicable to the driver of a vehicle, except as to special regulations of I.C.
9-21 to provisions of I.C. 9-21-11 which by their nature can have no
application.
(Prior Code, § 73.03) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.04 PARKING BICYCLE.
   No person shall park a bicycle upon any highway other than upon the roadway
against the curb, upon the sidewalk in a rack to support the bicycle, against a
building, or at the curb in any manner to afford the least obstruction to
pedestrian traffic.
(Prior Code, § 73.04) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.05 SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   No person shall operate a bicycle upon a sidewalk within a business
district.
   (B)   Any person operating a bicycle upon a sidewalk shall yield the right-
of-way to any pedestrian and give an audible signal before passing the
pedestrian.
(Prior Code, § 73.05) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.06 ROADWAYS AND BICYCLE PATHS.
   (A)   Every person operating a bicycle upon a roadway shall ride as near to
the right side of the roadway as practicable, exercising due care when passing
a standing vehicle or one proceeding in the same direction.
   (B)   Every person operating a bicycle shall use any designated bicycle path
which has been provided adjacent to a roadway, and not the roadway itself.
(Prior Code, § 73.06) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.07 ATTACHMENT TO POLES.
   No bicycle operator shall park a bicycle near or secure it to any publicly-
owned pole or post for more than 12 consecutive hours. No bicycle operator
shall park a bicycle near or secure it to any publicly-owned pole or post if an
official traffic-control device or signal prohibits parking or securing
bicycles at that location. No bicycle operator shall secure any bicycle to any
tree, fire hydrant, police or fire call box, or so that it impedes the normal
and reasonable movement of pedestrians or other traffic.
(Prior Code, § 73.07) (Ord. 104-81, passed 5-21-1981) Penalty, see §
73.99
SNOWMOBILES
§ 73.20 OPERATING RULES.
   Every snowmobile operator shall stop at all intersecting streets and keep
the vehicle headlight on at all times.
(Prior Code, § 73.20) (Ord. 337-1979, passed 11-26-1979) Penalty, see §
73.99
Cross-reference:
   Locations in which snowmobiles are prohibited, see
Ch. 74, Sch. III
§ 73.21 RECKLESSNESS PROHIBITED.
   No person shall operate any snowmobile in a reckless or unsafe manner.
(Prior Code, § 73.21) (Ord. 337-1979, passed 11-26-1979) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.22 SCHOOLS, PARKS, AND CEMETERIES.
   No person shall operate any snowmobile on any property owned by the high
school, elementary school, or in any cemetery or town property.
(Prior Code, § 73.22) (Ord. 337-1979, passed 11-26-1979) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.23 OPERATING HOURS.
   No person shall operate any snowmobile between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and
6:00 a.m. except during emergencies as proclaimed by the Council.
(Prior Code, § 73.23) (Ord. 337-1979, passed 11-26-1979) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.24 MINIMUM AGE.
   No person under 14 years of age shall operate a snowmobile without the
immediate supervision of a parent or legal guardian except on property owned by
that person’s parents or legal guardian.
(Prior Code, § 73.24) (Ord. 337-1979, passed 11-26-1979) Penalty, see §
73.99
ALTERNATIVE VEHICLES
§ 73.35 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, ALTERNATE TRANSPORTATION is described as
any motorized vehicle not requiring to be plated, above 50cc, including but not
limited to electric vehicles, golf carts, and utility carts; excluding quad
runners and dirt bikes, which are off-road vehicles only.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008; Ord. 104-09, passed 9-14-2009)
§ 73.36 OPERATOR REQUIREMENTS; MINIMUM AGE.
   Any operator of alternative transportation must be a licensed driver under
state law, have such license on his or her person at all times, and comply with
local and state ordinance and laws.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008; Ord. 104-09, passed 9-14-2009) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.37 OPERATING HOURS.
   Operation of such alternative transportation shall be during daylight hours
only, with the exception that units equipped with head lights, tail lights, and
turn signals may be driven after dark on the town streets. Operation is not
permitted on county roads or state highways.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008: Ord. 104-09, passed 9-14-2009) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.38 REQUIRED EQUIPMENT; PASSENGER RESTRICTIONS.
   Any alternative transportation requires rearview mirrors and a slow-moving
vehicle sign. Restrict limit of passengers to the number of seats on each unit
(example: bench seats, two passengers only, and the like).
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.39 INSPECTIONS.
   (A)   Any alternative transportation vehicle or device must be inspected on
an annual basis. The inspection cost shall be $25 per unit to be payable to the
Town of Frankton, 108 E. Sigler Street, Frankton, IN 46044, and the inspection
shall be conducted by the Police Department. The purpose of the inspection will
be to determine if the unit is safe and in compliance with all related rules
and regulations.
   (B)   The above described inspections will be conducted by appointments
scheduled in advance by the owner. For appointments, contact the Police
Department at (765) 754-8833.
   (C)   The owner must renew the inspection on or before May 1 annually by
scheduling an appointment as described above. The Clerk’s office will maintain
records of inspection.
   (D)   The Police Department reserves the right to remove any uninspected or
unsafe alternative transportation unit.
   (E)   The Town Clerk or a designated representative will affix an inspection
sticker to the front driver’s side of each unit after a satisfactory inspection
has been completed, along with proof of insurance to be filed and recorded.
   (F)   If an alternative transportation vehicle is sold, the inspection
sticker is void. The new owner must have the vehicle inspected and follow all
rules and regulations as described above.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008; Ord. 100-13, passed 3-8-2013) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.40 INSURANCE.
   Each alternative transportation unit must have liability insurance, and
verification of that insurance must be presented at the time of the above
referenced inspection and carried while operating such vehicle.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008) Penalty, see §
73.99
§ 73.41 PARKING.
   Parking will be parallel to, and on the right in, the same direction as the
traffic is designed to flow.
(Ord. 102-08, passed 3-10-2008)
§ 73.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
70.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating §§
71.01 through 71.07 may have his or her bicycle impounded for not more than 30
days. Any person violating §§
71.01 through 71.07 shall be fined $5.
   (C)   Any person violating any provision of §§
71.20 through
71.24 shall be fined not less than $50, nor more than $100.
(Prior Code, § 73.99)
   (D)   Any violators of §§
73.35 through
73.41 will be cited for local ordinance violation and fined $50 for the first
offense, $75 for the second offense, and $100 for the third offense. Additional
offenses will result in suspension of use on public roadways for the remainder
of the year.

   Chapter

CHAPTER 90: ABANDONED VEHICLES
Section
   
90.01   Definition
   
90.02   Notice
   
90.03   Report and removal
   
90.04   Reclaiming a vehicle; public sale
   
90.05   Abandoned vehicle fund; limitation of costs
§ 90.01 DEFINITION.
   (A)   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   (B)   For the purposes of this section, and in accordance with I.C. 9-13-2-
1, ABANDONED VEHICLE shall mean:
      (1)   A vehicle located on public property illegally;
      (2)   A vehicle left on public property without being moved for 24 hours;
      (3)   A vehicle located on public property in such a manner as to
constitute a hazard or obstruction to the movement of pedestrian or vehicular
traffic on a public right-of-way;
      (4)   A vehicle that has remained on private property without the consent
of the owner or person in control of that property for more than 48 hours;
      (5)   A vehicle from which the engine, transmission, or differential has
been removed or that is otherwise partially dismantled or inoperable and left
on public property;
      (6)   A vehicle that has been removed by a towing service or public
agency upon request of an officer enforcing a statute or an ordinance other
than I.C. 9-13-2 et seq. if the impounded vehicle is not claimed or redeemed by
the owner or the owner’s agent within 20 days after the vehicle’s removal; or
      (7)   A vehicle that is at least three model years old, is mechanically
inoperable, and is left on private property continuously in a location visible
from public property for more than 20 days. For the purposes of this division
(B)(7), a vehicle covered by a tarpaulin or other plastic, vinyl, rubber,
cloth, or textile covering is considered to be visible.
   (C)   Notwithstanding the foregoing, the phrase ABANDONED VEHICLE shall not
include:
      (1)   A vehicle in operable condition specifically adapted or constructed
for operation on privately owned raceways;
      (2)   A vehicle stored as the property of a member of the armed forces of
the United States who is on active duty assignment;
      (3)   A vehicle located on a vehicle sale lot;
      (4)   A vehicle located upon property licensed or zoned as an automobile
scrap yard;
      (5)   A vehicle registered and licensed under I.C. 9-18.1-5-5 as an
antique vehicle; or
      (6)   A golf cart or off-road vehicle.
(Ord. 104-13, passed 9-9-2013)
§ 90.02 NOTICE.
   A Town Marshal, Deputy Marshal, or reserve officer of the town (each
collectively referred to herein as an “officer”) who finds or is notified of a
vehicle or parts believed to be abandoned shall attach in a prominent place a
notice tag containing the following information:
   (A)   The date, time, officer’s name, town, and address and telephone number
to contact for information;
   (B)   The vehicle or parts are considered abandoned;
   (C)   The vehicle or parts will be removed after:
      (1)   Twenty-four hours if the vehicle is located on or within the right-
of-way of an interstate highway or any highway that is designated as part of
the state highway system under I.C. 8-23-4; or
      (2)   Seventy-two hours for any other vehicle.
   (D)   The person who owns the vehicle will be held responsible for all costs
incidental to the removal, storage, and disposal of the vehicle; and
   (E)   The person who owns the vehicle may avoid costs by removal of the
vehicle or parts within:
      (1)   Twenty-four hours if the vehicle is located on or within the right-
of-way of an interstate highway or any highway that is designated as part of
the state highway system under I.C. 8-23-4; or
      (2)   Seventy-two hours for any other vehicle.
(Ord. 104-13, passed 9-9-2013)
§ 90.03 REPORT AND REMOVAL.
   (A)   If the vehicle or part tagged under §
90.02 is not removed within the applicable period, the officer shall prepare a
written abandoned vehicle report of the vehicle or parts, including information
on the condition and missing parts. Photographs may be taken to describe the
condition of the vehicle or parts. The owner of an abandoned vehicle or parts
shall be responsible for the abandonment and liable for all of the costs
incidental to the removal, storage, and disposal in accordance with I.C. 9-22-
1-4.
   (B)   In accordance with I.C. 9-22-1-13, if the vehicle is a junk vehicle
and the market value of the abandoned vehicle or parts is less than $1,000, and
after the applicable notice period described in §
90.02, a towing service shall immediately transfer the vehicle to a storage
yard. A copy of the abandoned vehicle report and photographs, if any, relating
to the abandoned vehicle shall be provided to the storage yard. The towing
service or storage yard may dispose of such an abandoned vehicle, and maintain
records and provide any required notice to the Bureau of Motor Vehicles of the
same, in accordance with I.C. 9-22-1-13. This division (B) shall not be
construed as the adoption of an ordinance by the town in accordance with I.C.
9-22-l-13(b).
   (C)   If, in the opinion of the officer, the market value of the abandoned
vehicle or parts is at least $1,000; the officer, before placing the notice tag
described in §
90.02 on the vehicle or parts, shall make a reasonable effort to ascertain the
person who owns the vehicle or parts or who may be in control of the vehicle or
parts. After 72 hours, the officer shall require the vehicle or parts to be
towed to a storage yard.
   (D)   Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in divisions (B) and (C)
above, within 72 hours after removal of a vehicle to a storage yard or towing
service, the towing service shall conduct the search and provide the
notification prescribed by I.C. 9-22-1-19. However, if the vehicle or parts are
in such a condition that vehicle identification numbers or other means of
identification are not available to determine the person who owns or holds a
lien on the vehicle, the vehicle may be disposed of without notice to the
extent allowed by law.
   (E)   The storage yard or towing service storing a vehicle described in
division (C) above shall notify the town if the person who owns or holds a lien
upon the vehicle does not appear within 20 days after the notice described by
division (D) above, and the town may sell the vehicle or parts to the highest
bidder at a public sale after providing notice of the sale in accordance with
I.C. 5-3-1, except that only one newspaper insertion one week before the public
sale shall be required. A bill of sale shall be provided in accordance with
I.C. 9-22-1-24.
(Ord. 104-13, passed 9-9-2013)
§ 90.04 RECLAIMING A VEHICLE; PUBLIC SALE.
   (A)   Notwithstanding anything provided in §
90.03(B) through (E), if the properly identified person who owns or holds a
lien on a vehicle appears at the site of storage before the disposal of the
vehicle or parts and pays all costs incurred against the vehicle or parts at
that time, the vehicle or parts shall be released, and the towing or storage
service shall immediately notify the town of such release.
   (B)   Such notification shall include the name, signature, and address of
the person that owns or holds the lien on the vehicle, a description of the
vehicle or parts, costs, and the date of release.
(Ord. 104-13, passed 9-9-2013)
§ 90.05 ABANDONED VEHICLE FUND; LIMITATION OF COSTS.
   (A)   An abandoned vehicle fund is hereby established in accordance with
I.C. 9-22-1-30. The Council shall annually appropriate sufficient money to the
abandoned vehicle fund to carry out the purposes of the ordinance codified
herein, and the money remaining in the abandoned vehicle fund at the end of the
year shall remain in the abandoned vehicle fund and shall not revert to the
general fund.
   (B)   The town hereby establishes a towing charge of $75 and a storage
charge of $25 per day (not to exceed $1,500), which shall be filed with the
Bureau of Motor Vehicles. The costs for removal and storage of an abandoned
vehicle or parts not claimed by the person who owns or holds a lien on the
vehicle shall be paid from the abandoned vehicle fund; however, the proceeds of
the sale of an abandoned vehicle or parts shall be credited against the costs
of removal, storage, and disposal of the vehicle. All other costs incurred by
the town in administering this chapter shall be paid from the abandoned vehicle
fund.
   (C)   The proceeds from the sale of abandoned vehicles or parts shall be
deposited into the town’s abandoned vehicle fund.
(Ord. 104-13, passed 9-9-2013)
CHAPTER 91: ANIMALS
Section
   
91.01   Definitions
   
91.02   Vaccinations
   
91.03   Rabies
   
91.04   Restraint
   
91.05   Livestock prohibition
   
91.06   Stray animals
 
   
91.99   Penalty
§ 91.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   ANIMAL. Any live, nonhuman vertebrate creature, domestic or wild.
   AT LARGE. Not under restraint.
   DOMESTIC ANIMAL. A domestic animal, as defined in I.C. 15-2.1-2-15.
   HARBORING. Permitting any animal to remain or to be fed within any person’s
enclosure, place of business, or residence, or any premises controlled by that
person for more than three consecutive days.
   LIVESTOCK. All horses, cows, goats, swine, fowl, sheep, and all related
types of animals and offspring, and any animals weighing 200 pounds or more.
LIVESTOCK shall not include any animals harbored or kept for exhibition
purposes for less than two weeks during any six-month period.
   OWNER. Any person harboring any animal.
   PERSON. A person, as defined in I.C. 15-2.1-2-39.
   PUBLIC NUISANCE. Any animal which:
      (1)   Is “at large”;
      (2)   Attacks or molests other animals, humans, or passing vehicles;
      (3)   Damages property; or
      (4)   Disturbs a reasonable person with continuous or excessive noise.
   RESTRAINT. Confinement within the property of the owner or securing by a
lead or leash.
   STRAY. Any animal whose owner cannot be identified following reasonable
inquiry.
   VICIOUS ANIMAL. Any animal constituting an immediate and serious danger to
humans or to other animals.
(Prior Code, § 91.01)
Statutory reference:
   Animal health, see I.C. 15-2.1-1-1
   Definition of domestic animal, see I.C. 15-2.1-2-15
   Definition of person, see I.C. 15-2.1-2-39
§ 91.02 VACCINATIONS.
   No person shall harbor any cat or dog which is six-months old or older and
which has not been vaccinated against rabies. A rabies vaccination tag issued
by a licensed veterinarian, an identification tag designating the owner of the
animal and the owner’s address, and a dog tag issued by the County Assessor’s
Office shall be deemed proof of compliance with this section.
(Prior Code, § 91.02) Penalty, see §
91.99
Statutory reference:
   Immunization certificates, see I.C. 15-2.1-6-5
§ 91.03 RABIES.
   (A)   Any domestic animal which has bitten a person shall be impounded at
the expense of the owner for a period of not less than 14 days and until the
County Health Officer can determine if the animal is rabid. If the animal dies
while impounded, a test shall be conducted at the expense of the owner to
determine if the animal was rabid. The death of any animal suspected to have
been rabid shall be reported to the County Health Officer immediately following
the discovery of its death.
   (B)   Any domestic animal which has been bitten by an animal suspected to be
rabid shall be confined for a period of six months at the expense of the owner
or shall be destroyed.
   (C)   No person who knows or suspects that an animal under his or her
control is rabid shall allow that animal to leave his or her control except to
be taken to the City of Anderson Animal Shelter or a veterinarian. Any person
who knows or suspects that an animal is rabid shall immediately notify the
Police Department or the County Health Officer.
(Prior Code, § 91.03) Penalty, see §
91.99
Statutory reference:
   Local animal control ordinances, see I.C. 15-2.1-6-13
§ 91.04 RESTRAINT.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person being the owner of or having the
care, custody, or control of any dog to allow, suffer, or permit such dog to be
at large upon the streets or other public places in the town or in or on any
other place beyond the premises of such person, unless such dog be on a leash
or otherwise under the complete control of a person capable of controlling such
dog.
   (B)   The Police Department shall order the confinement of any animal
determined by the Department to be vicious. Any vicious animal shall be
confined within a building or secure enclosure by its owner and shall be caged
or securely muzzled unless on the property of its owner.
   (C)   In cases that a dog is transported to the Animal Protection League by
the Police Department, the town will pay a per-incident fee on a monthly basis
to the Animal Protection League for cases involving dog bites and/or if the
Police Department is unable to identify a proper address for a loose dog. The
Police Department shall comply with paper work procedures required by the
facility.
(Prior Code, § 91.04) (Ord. 227, passed 5-4-1964; Ord. 104-08, passed 2-11-
2008; Ord. 101-12, passed 4-9-2012) Penalty, see §
91.99
Statutory reference:
   Animals running at large, see I.C. 15-2.1-21-8
§ 91.05 LIVESTOCK PROHIBITION.
   No livestock shall be harbored or kept in the town.
(Prior Code, § 91.05) (Ord. 23, passed 8-24-1996) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.06 STRAY ANIMALS.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      ANIMAL. Any live, non-human vertebrae creature, domestic or wild.
      ANIMAL CONTROL OFFICER. The person(s) employed by the town whose duties
shall include, among others, the enforcement of the provisions included in this
section.
      ANIMAL SHELTER. The premises contracted with by the town for the purpose
of caring for animals which are at large, lost, or otherwise homeless.
      AT LARGE. Any animal shall be deemed AT LARGE when it is not under
restraint.
      ATTACK. Any behavior by an animal that constitutes an immediate and
serious physical threat to human beings or other animals, whether or not such
behavior results in injury to such human beings or other animals. This behavior
includes, but is not limited to, snapping at, lunging at, and/or attempting to
bite human beings or animals.
      HARBORING. The actions of any person which permit any animal habitually
to remain, lodge, or to be fed within his home, store, enclosure, yard, or
place of business or any premises on which a person resides or controls, shall
be considered HARBORING an animal. An animal shall be presumed HARBORED if it
is fed or sheltered for three consecutive days.
      LIVESTOCK. All horses, cows, goats, swine, fowl, and sheep, all related
types of animals and offspring, and any animals weighing 200 pounds or more.
LIVESTOCK shall not include any animals harbored for exhibition purposes for
less than two weeks during any six-month period.
      OWNER. Any person harboring any animal.
      PERSON. A person, as defined in I.C. 15-17-2-83.
      PUBLIC NUISANCE. Any animal(s) which:
         (a)   Interferes with passers-by or passing vehicles;
         (b)   Attacks other animals;
         (c)   Is repeatedly at large; or
         (d)   Damages public or private property.
      RESTRAINT. Any animal secured by a leash and lead within the real
property limits of its owner. Any animals not physically confined to the
owner’s property shall be presumed not to be under RESTRAINT.
      STRAY. Any animal which doesn’t appear, upon reasonable inquiry, to have
an owner.
      VETERINARY HOSPITAL. Any establishment maintained and operated by a
veterinarian for surgery, diagnosis, and treatment of diseases and injuries of
animals.
      VICIOUS ANIMALS. Any animal that, by its behavior, constitutes an
immediate and serious physical threat to human beings or animals.
   (B)   Authorization of the Clerk-Treasurer to enter into agreements.
      (1)   The Clerk-Treasurer is hereby authorized and directed, on behalf of
the town, to negotiate and enter into a contract or agreement with one or more
organizations qualified to provide animal impoundment services, including, but
not limited to, the Animal Protection League (APL) or the Elwood Animal Shelter
(the “agreement”).
      (2)   Such agreement shall provide for the removal, collection, and care
of stray or abandoned cats, found within the corporate limits of the town. The
Agreement may include terms governing pickup procedures, sheltering, veterinary
care, spaying/neutering, and any other related services.
      (3)   The Clerk-Treasurer shall support payment for the agreement by a
fully itemized invoice or bill and certification.
      (4)   The Town Council shall review and allow the claim at the body’s
next regular or special meeting following the preapproved payment of the
expense.
      (5)   The Clerk-Treasurer is hereby authorized to enter into the
agreement at an amount that shall not exceed $9,000 in any calendar year
without further approval from the town.
   (C)   Restraint of animals.
      (1)   All animals shall be kept under restraint. No owner shall fail to
exercise due care and control of his or her animals to prevent them from
becoming a public nuisance. Every female animal in heat shall be confined in a
building or secured enclosure in a manner that the female animals cannot come
into contact with another animal or the same species except for planned
breeding. Every vicious animal, as determined by the Animal Control Officer,
shall be confined by the owner within a building or secured enclosure and shall
be securely muzzled or caged whenever off the premises of the owner.
      (2)   Any animal that is unrestrained or a public nuisance shall be taken
by the town police or Animal Control Officer and impounded in the Animal
Shelter, and there confined in a humane manner. If by a license tag or other
means, the owner of an impounded animal can be identified, the Animal Control
Officer shall immediately, upon impoundment, notify the owner by telephone or
mail. Upon identifying the dog owner, the town’s Police Department shall issue
a fine of $200 per incident, payable at the Frankton Town Hall, regardless of
whether a person has previously violated this section and regardless of whether
written notices or previous fines were issued. Any animal not re-claimed by its
owner within five working days shall become the property of the Animal Shelter
and shall be placed for adoption in a suitable home or humanely euthanized.
   (D)   Abandonment. It shall be unlawful for a person to knowingly,
intentionally, or recklessly abandon a domestic animal or livestock as
described by this section.
(Ord. 103-25, passed - -2025) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any person violating or failing to comply with the provisions of
§
91.04(A) and (C) shall, upon first offense, receive written notice from the
Council of failure to comply. A second offense will result in violation of
local ordinance and a fine of $25, with each offense thereafter to increase by
an additional $25. Fines will be payable to the Town of Frankton, 108 E. Sigler
Street.
      (2)   In cases described in §
91.04(C), upon identifying the dog owner or property owner, the Police
Department shall issue a fine of $200 per incident, payable at the Town Hall.
This fine is due regardless of whether a person has previously violated §
91.04(A) or (C) and regardless of whether written notices or previous fines
were issued.
(Prior Code, § 91.99)
   (C)   (1)   The owner of a domestic animal found to be roaming at large or
stray, per §
91.06, may be fined $50 for the first offense and $100 for each subsequent
offense involving the same animal occurring within one year.
      (2)   For any other violation of §
91.06, the owner may be fined up to $200.
      (3)   Fines will be payable to the “Town of Frankton, 108 E. Sigler
Street”.

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 92.01 FIREWORKS.
   (A)   Recitals. The recitals stated in the ordinance codified herein are
hereby incorporated by reference.
   (B)   Permitted hours of use. The use of consumer fireworks in the town is
hereby permitted only during the following dates and times as set forth below:
      (1)   Between the hours of 5:00 p.m. and two hours after sunset on June
29, June 30, July 1, July 2, July 3, July 5, July 6, July 7, July 8, and July
9;
      (2)   Between the hours of 10:00 a.m. and 12:00 a.m. (midnight) on July
4; and
      (3)   Between the hours of 10:00 a.m. on December 31 and 1:00 a.m. on
January 1.
   (C)   Locations. The igniting, discharging, or use of consumer fireworks in
the public right-of- way or street is strictly prohibited.
   (D)   Conflict with state law. No provision of this section is intended to
conflict with or supersede state law.
(Ord. 104-22, passed 8-8-2022)

OUTDOOR HEATERS
§ 92.35 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CLEAN NATURAL WOOD. Natural wood which:
      (1)   Has not been pressure treated, painted, varnished, or coated with a
similar material;
      (2)   Does not contain resins or glues, such as plywood or other
composite wood products; and
      (3)   Is not particle board, “refuse”, or “yard waste”.
   NUISANCE. The burning of any material of any kind which generates smoke or
odor from an outdoor hydronic heater so as to endanger the public or in a
matter that unreasonably disturbs the comfort of any person residing in the
vicinity.
   OUTDOOR HYDRONIC HEATER. Any fuel-burning device designed to burn wood or
other approved renewable solid fuels that is intended for outdoor installation
or installation in structures not normally occupied by humans and that heats
building space or water (or both) by the distribution of a fluid heated in the
device.
   REFUSE and YARD WASTE. Any waste material including, but not limited to:
      (1)   Leaves, grass, garbage, and other similar organic material;
      (2)   Construction and demolition waste material;
      (3)   Automobiles or parts thereof;
      (4)   Flammable (excluding clean natural wood), toxic, or explosive
material;
      (5)   Electrical wiring; and
      (6)   Rubber and plastic products.
(Ord. 100-18, passed 1-8-2018)
§ 92.36 INSTALLATION AND USE.
   An outdoor hydronic heater may be installed and used within the town only in
accordance with the following provisions.
   (A)   An outdoor hydronic heater may use as a fuel source only clean natural
wood; provided, however, that other fuel sources expressly recommended by the
manufacturer of a particular model of outdoor hydronic heater may be used with
that model if they are not specifically prohibited by this subchapter.
      (1)   Refuse and/or yard waste shall not be used as a fuel source for
outdoor hydronic heaters.
      (2)   No material shall be used as a fuel source that causes any
particulate matter that is detrimental to human beings or animals to be
released into the air.
   (B)   No outdoor hydronic heater shall be utilized in a manner that
endangers the public, threatens the safety or health of the public, or
constitutes a nuisance as defined in §
92.35 or otherwise defined by law.
   (C)   An outdoor hydronic heater shall be located to the rear or the side of
the structure for which it is intended to act as a heat source and shall be
located at least 20 feet from the nearest structure on the same property, 50
feet from any property line, 100 feet from the nearest non-residential
structure on an adjoining property, and 200 feet from the nearest residence
that is not located on the same property.
   (D)   An outdoor hydronic heater shall have an attached permanent chimney
that extends at least five feet higher than the height of the roofs of all
residences located within 150 feet of the unit and not located on the same
property as the unit. The town, by its authorized representative(s), may
approve in writing a lesser chimney height on a case-by-case basis if necessary
to comply with the manufacturer’s recommendations, provided the outdoor
hydronic heater does not or will not create a nuisance and provided that the
outdoor hydronic heater meets IDEM emissions requirements in 326 I.A.C. 4-3-3.
Such approval may be revoked by the town if it causes the outdoor hydronic
heater to create a nuisance.
   (E)   An outdoor hydronic heater shall be UL listed and have attached an
approved U.S. Environmental Protection Agency (“EPA”) label indicating the
model of the unit and that the unit meets all EPA rules and regulations in
force at the time of installation.
   (F)   An outdoor hydronic heater must be installed and operated at all times
in accordance with the manufacturer’s instructions and recommendations. In the
event of a conflict between this subchapter and the manufacturer’s
instructions, the requirements of this subchapter shall apply unless the
manufacturer’s instructions are stricter, in which case the manufacturer’s
instructions shall apply.
   (G)   No outdoor hydronic heater may be operated between the dates of May 1
and September 30 of each calendar year.
   (H)   An outdoor hydronic heater must meet IDEM’s emissions requirements in
326 I.A.C. 4-3-3 and 4-3-5(e), which are hereby incorporated as part of this
code of ordinances as if fully set forth herein.
(Ord. 100-18, passed 1-8-2018) Penalty, see §
92.99
§ 92.37 PERMITS.
   (A)   Prior to the installation and operation of any outdoor hydronic
heater, a permit must be obtained from the town. The cost of such permit shall
be $50. Failure to obtain any permit before installation will subject an
individual to fines and penalties and a nuisance action as described herein.
   (B)   At the time of permit application, the applicant must provide the town
with a plot map showing all buildings and property lines within 200 feet of the
anticipated placement of the outdoor hydronic heater, as well as a copy of the
manufacturer’s specifications and recommendations.
(Ord. 100-18, passed 1-8-2018)
§ 92.38 GRANDFATHER PROVISIONS.
   (A)   The lawful use of a non-conforming outdoor hydronic heater existing at
the time of the adoption or amendment of the ordinance codified herein may be
continued, although the use of such outdoor hydronic heaters does not conform
to the provisions of this subchapter.
   (B)   Notwithstanding the foregoing, a non-conforming outdoor hydronic
heater shall not be used or operated in such a manner as to constitute a
nuisance or cause a condition hazardous to human health or prohibited by any
applicable federal, state or local law, regulation, or ordinance. Additionally,
any such non-conforming outdoor hydronic heaters shall be used and maintained
in compliance with the provisions of subchapter to the maximum extent
reasonably possible.
   (C)   Outdoor hydronic heaters that are relocated shall be considered newly
installed units and shall be subject to all provisions and requirements of this
subchapter. All existing outdoor hydronic heaters must be registered with the
town within 90 days after the adoption of the ordinance codified herein.
Failure to do so will result in the loss of such outdoor hydronic heaters’
grandfathered status, and the same will be subject to all provisions and
requirements of this subchapter.
(Ord. 100-18, passed 1-8-2018)
§ 92.39 ENFORCEMENT.
   Any authorized officer, agent, employee, or representative of the town who
presents credentials may inspect any property for the purposes of ascertaining
compliance with this subchapter.
(Ord. 100-18, passed 1-8-2018)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Any burning which violates any provision of this section
constitutes a public nuisance. Any person who violates any provision of the
sections mentioned above shall be fined not more than $2,500.
(Prior Code, § 92.99)
      (2)   The Fire or Police Department of the town shall issue a warning
notice to a first- time violator of §§
92.16,
92.19, and/or
92.20. The person must then correct the violation by immediately extinguishing
the fire. Failure or refusal to immediately extinguish the fire shall result in
a citation being issued. Issuance of a citation to the violator shall result in
the imposition of a $25 fine.
   (C)   Any person or entity who violates any of the provisions of §§
92.36 through
92.38 shall have committed a civil infraction and shall be subject to a fine(s)
in accordance with the schedule below. Each day that a violation of §§
92.36 through
92.38 continues unabated constitutes a separate offense. In addition, any
violation of §§
92.36 through
92.38 shall be deemed a nuisance, and the town or its officers, agents, or
representatives may bring an action in any court of competent jurisdiction to
abate said nuisance.

CHAPTER 93: STREETS, SIDEWALKS, AND PUBLIC PROPERTY
Section
   
93.01   Streets to be clear
   
93.02   Sidewalk maintenance; repairs
   
93.03   Basketball goal placement
   
93.04   Right-of-way application fee
§ 93.01 STREETS TO BE CLEAR.
   Nothing is to be placed in a town street or alleyway which may even
temporarily impede traffic at any time through any street or alley.
(Prior Code, § 93.01) (Ord. 192-89, passed 9-11-1989) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 93.02 SIDEWALK MAINTENANCE; REPAIRS.
   (A)   The responsibility for the care, maintenance, and repairs of sidewalks
within the town is hereby deemed that of the property owners abutting any
sidewalk. Property owners abutting any sidewalk shall keep the sidewalk in
reasonably safe condition and shall maintain and repair the sidewalk at their
own expense as and when needed or within 30 days of being notified by the town
that the sidewalk is in need of repairs.
   (B)   The Council hereby establishes a sidewalk repair program whereby the
town shall provide assistance to owners of residential property within the town
to maintain and repair the sidewalks located upon their properties in
accordance with the following rules and requirements.
      (1)   Applications for sidewalk repair assistance shall be accepted by
the town during the months of April through September from residential property
owners within the town.
      (2)   The town shall provide sidewalk repair assistance in accordance
with this section on a first-come, first-served basis with respect to the
submission of applications for sidewalk repair assistance.
      (3)   Upon the depletion of funds appropriated for sidewalk-repair
assistance for the calendar year, any remaining applications shall be tabled
until the following calendar year.
      (4)   If the town receives more applications during a calendar year than
funds that are available, the town reserves the right to prioritize
applications on the basis of safety, volume of traffic, length of sidewalk
needing repaired, and aesthetics.
      (5)   Upon receipt of an application for sidewalk repair assistance, the
town shall inspect the sidewalk to be repaired and engage a contractor to
provide an estimate to the town and the property owner of the cost of labor and
materials to repair the sidewalk.
      (6)   Repairs on the sidewalk may be made under this sidewalk repair
program only after the following have been completed:
         (a)   Both the town and the property owner approve the estimate for
repairs provided by the contractor; and
         (b)   The property owner completes and files with the town a written
acknowledgment that the property owner is responsible for 50% of the actual
costs of the repairs and deposits with the town an amount equal to 50% of the
estimate for repairs provided by the contractor, such amount to be deposited in
the town’s Sidewalk Repair Fund.
      (7)   Following completion of the repairs and the town’s approval of the
repairs, the town shall pay the cost of the repairs to the contractor from the
Sidewalk Repair Fund.
   (C)   This sidewalk repair program in no way relieves the responsibility of
property owners to maintain and repair sidewalks abutting their property in
accordance with division (A) above and is intended only as a mechanism by which
property owners may request assistance of the town in fulfilling such
responsibility.
(Ord. 103-17, passed 7-10-2013)
§ 93.03 BASKETBALL GOAL PLACEMENT.
   (A)   A basketball goal, whether permanent or portable, shall be located in
the property owner’s driveway, backyard, garage, or lawn, provided that said
location is otherwise in accordance with the town’s ordinances and resolutions.
   (B)   If a basketball goal is placed on the resident’s driveway, the
following rules apply.
      (1)   No basketball goal is to extend out into the street, including the
hoop. A placement of the basketball goal in a driveway must be a minimum of
three feet from the roadway.
      (2)   Except as otherwise provided herein, a basketball goal is not to be
placed on a sidewalk or public right-of-way.
      (3)   In the case of a dead-end street or cul-de-sac, a portable
basketball goal may be temporarily placed at the end of the street or cul-de-
sac within the public right-of-way, provided that such portable basketball goal
must be removed from the public right-of-way when not in use.
   (C)   If any basketball goal is located in violation of this section, the
town shall have the right to remove the basketball goal after providing the
owner with ten days’ written notice.
      (1)   Any basketball goal removed by the town shall be claimed within ten
days, otherwise the basketball goal will be disposed of.
      (2)   If the owner or person responsible for the basketball goal wishes
it returned prior to its disposal, a fee of $25 shall be charged for the town’s
removal and storage of the basketball goal.
(Ord. 103-22, passed 6-13-2022)
§ 93.04 RIGHT-OF-WAY APPLICATION FEE.
   A non-refundable application fee of $15 shall be submitted with each right-
of-way permit application. Applications received without the required fee shall
be deemed incomplete and shall not be processed until payment is received.
(Ord. 101-25, passed 6-18-2025)
CHAPTER 94: HEALTH AND SANITATION; NUISANCES
