GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 111.01 AUTHORITY TO REGULATE.
   The town may tax, license, and regulate transient merchants and charge a fee
for the transaction of business in the town and provide for the punishment of
violators.
(Prior Code, § 111.01) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897)
Statutory reference:
   Transient merchant tax, see I.C. 25-37-1-11
§ 111.02 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definition shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   TRANSIENT MERCHANTS. All persons designated TRANSIENT MERCHANTS under I.C.
25-37-1-2 et seq., as amended, but does not include solicitors.
(Prior Code, § 111.02) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897)
§ 111.03 PROHIBITIONS.
   (A)   No transient merchant shall remain upon any premises after being
requested to leave by any resident of the premises.
   (B)   No transient merchant shall conduct business or attempt to do so on
any property where a notice reading “no solicitors” (or words to that effect)
is posted.
   (C)   No transient merchant shall conduct business on Sunday or from one-
half hour before sunset until 8:00 a.m. A transient merchant may conduct
business at any time if he or she has a specific appointment with, or
invitation from, the prospective customer.
   (D)   No transient merchant shall engage in business without first obtaining
a permit to do so.
   (E)   No transient merchant shall refuse to display his or her permit upon
the request of any person.
(Prior Code, § 111.03) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.04 APPLICATION.
   (A)   Each applicant for a transient merchant permit shall complete and sign
an application stating the following information:
      (1)   The length of time for which he or she desires to conduct business;
      (2)   The local residence and mailing address of the applicant;
      (3)   The permanent residence and mailing address of the applicant;
      (4)   The name and the local and permanent mailing address of the person,
firm, corporation, or partnership (if any) which the applicant represents;
      (5)   The kind of business proposed to be conducted and the nature of the
goods, wares, merchandise, or services to be sold;
      (6)   If the applicant holds a driver’s license, any information
concerning that license deemed necessary by the Town Marshal; and
      (7)   Any other information deemed necessary by the Clerk-Treasurer.
   (B)   No applicant for a transient merchant permit shall give any false or
misleading information in the application.
(Prior Code, § 111.04) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.05 PERMIT.
   (A)   No transient merchant permit shall be issued by the Clerk-Treasurer
until the applicant has paid the fee set forth in the following schedule:
Duration of License Fee
Duration of License Fee
1 day               $1
2 days              $1.90
3 days              $2.75
4 days              $3.50
5 days              $4.25
1 week              $5
2 weeks             $10
3 weeks             $15
1 month             $20
2 months            $35
3 months            $50
6 months            $75
 
   (B)   No transient merchant permit shall be issued to a corporation,
partnership, or any entity except an individual person. Each individual
operating as a transient merchant shall obtain a permit, whether acting for
himself or herself or as an agent or representative of another.
   (C)   Every transient merchant permit shall:
      (1)   Be signed by the Clerk-Treasurer;
      (2)   State the date of its issuance; and
      (3)   State the date of its expiration.
   (D)   Any permit not stating the information in division (C) above or issued
in violation of any provision of this section is void.
(Prior Code, § 111.05) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897)
§ 111.06 REVOCATION.
   Any transient merchant permit may be revoked if the permittee violates any
provision of the State Transient Merchant Law, being I.C. 25-36-1-2, or this
section. The permit holder shall immediately surrender any revoked permit to
the Town Marshal.
(Prior Code, § 111.06) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897) Penalty, see §
111.99
FESTIVALS, PERSONAL SALES, AND THE LIKE
§ 111.20 AUTHORITY TO REGULATE.
   The town may tax, license, and regulate transient merchants, solicitors, and
peddlers (hereafter known as “applicants”) and charge a fee for the transaction
of business in the town and provide for punishment of violators.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022)
§ 111.21 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   PEDDLER. Any person who travels from place to place by any means carrying
goods for sale, or making sales, or making deliveries; or any person who,
without traveling from place to place, sells or offers goods for sale from any
public place within the town.
   SOLICITOR. Any person who travels by any means from place to place, taking
or attempting to take orders for sale of goods to be delivered in the future or
for services to be performed in the future. A person who is a SOLICITOR is not
a “peddler”.
   TRANSIENT MERCHANTS. Any person, whether as owner, agent, or consignee, who
engages in a temporary business of selling goods within the town and who, in
the furtherance of such business, uses any building, structure, vehicle, or any
place within the town.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022)
§ 111.22 APPLICATION; LICENSE.
   (A)   All applicants must file an application for a license with the Clerk-
Treasurer. The application shall state:
      (1)   The applicant’s name;
      (2)   Permanent address;
      (3)   Business name
      (4)   Proposed location within the town;
      (5)   Structure or vehicle from which products/merchandise are to be
sold;
      (6)   Type of business and/or products sold;
      (7)   Length of time for which the applicant desires to do business in
the town; and
      (8)   Any additional information the Clerk-Treasurer deems necessary.
   (B)   Businesses that propose to handle foodstuffs must attach with their
application the appropriate license from the County Health Department.
   (C)   The applicant must pay a licensing fee of $15 to the Clerk-Treasurer,
completing the application at least 30 days prior to the applicant’s intended
activities in the town.
   (D)   The Police Department shall conduct an investigation with a fee of $5.
If the application is not approved, the Clerk-Treasurer shall refund the $15.
The application shall be denied if the following are discovered during the
investigation.
      (1)   The applicant has been convicted of a crime of moral turpitude.
      (2)   The applicant has committed prior fraudulent acts.
      (3)   The applicant has made willful misstatements in the application.
      (4)   The applicant has committed prior violations of ordinances
pertaining to transient merchants, peddlers, solicitors and the like.
   (E)   The license will be good for 90 days after approval of the
application, and regardless of whether the applicant conducts business in the
town, the $15 license fee will not be refunded.
   (F)   The license issued shall be posted conspicuously in or at the place
named therein. If there is more than one location within the town, a separate
license will be issued for each location.
   (G)   The applicant may extend the license for an additional 90 days by
paying $5 to the Clerk- Treasurer to cover the additional administrative costs
associated with the extended license.
   (H)   If the applicant conducts door-to-door sales, the license must be
displayed on the outermost layer of clothing worn or displayed on the vehicle
and identifying the name of the company or organization represented.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.23 SPECIAL EVENTS.
   (A)   The Heritage Days Festival, Town Market, and specified other events
that may occur during the course of the year will be managed by a board or
committee that sets the rules and regulations for such event and will not be
sponsored by the town.
   (B)   Each festival or event will be held in a designated area under the
board or committee’s control. All rules and regulations of these festivals and
events must be within the existing laws of the state and regulations of the
town.
   (C)   To promote any festival or event, the Council strongly recommends that
all vendors work with the committee in charge of the festival or event for a
unified fundraiser and set up within the designated area.
   (D)   The Police Department may, with the approval of the Council, close off
a street or alley as required or necessary during any event or festival
described in this section.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.24 GARAGE OR YARD SALES.
   (A)   A GARAGE SALE or YARD SALE is defined as a public or private sale
conducted by the owner or occupier of a premise, and conducted within a
residence, garage, or other accessory buildings or outside thereof, which sale
is of six or more items of personal property owned or in the possession of the
owner or occupier of the premises, which personal property was not acquired by
the owner or occupier for the purpose of resale.
   (B)   A garage or yard sale may be conducted no more than three times in any
one calendar year on any premises located in any R1, R2, or R3 zones, but no
such sale shall be conducted for more than three consecutive days.
   (C)   Such garage or yard sale shall only be conducted during the hours from
sunrise to sunset.
   (D)   All personal property exhibited for sale outside any structure during
such garage or yard sale shall be removed from the outside and placed within a
structure immediately following the last day of such sale. All signs erected
for such garage or yard sale shall likewise be removed.
   (E)   Garage or yard sales sponsored by the town or the Heritage Days
Festival will not count toward the maximum allowed in one calendar year.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.25 EXEMPTIONS.
   Situations that are exempt from these regulations set forth in this
subchapter include:
   (A)   An individual while (and to the extent) he or she is engaged in a
protected political speech or activity;
   (B)   An individual while (and to the extend) he or she is engaged in a
protected religious speech or activity;
   (C)   An individual engaged in the solicitation of funds and/or the sale of
items on behalf of a not-for-profit or nonprofit organization or association
that is exempt from the state’s Gross Retail Tax;
   (D)   An individual who, due to pre-exemption or applicable federal or state
law, is exempt from local licensing requirements;
   (E)   An individual who has been invited by the lawful owner or occupant of
a residence or business to solicit his or her particular goods or services to
that property owner or resident; and
   (F)   Any person engaged in sales or offers to sell during any event that is
pre-approved by the Council.
(Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-2022)
§ 111.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating any provision of §§
111.03 through
111.06 shall be fined not more than $25 for each offense. Every day a violation
continues constitutes a separate offense.
   (C)   Any person or business, either as principal or agent, who engages in
business in violation of §§
111.22 through
111.24 will be fined an amount not to exceed $40 but no less than $5 per day
that such violation occurs or continues.
(Prior Code, § 111.99) (Ord. 26, passed 2-22-1897; Ord. 105-22, passed 6-13-
2022)
TITLE XIII: GENERAL OFFENSES
   Chapter
      
130.   PROHIBITED BEHAVIOR
CHAPTER 130: PROHIBITED BEHAVIOR
Section
   
130.01   Curfews
   
130.02   Firearms
 
   
130.99   Penalty
§ 130.01 CURFEWS.
   (A)   The Council determines that the curfew time established by I.C. 31-6-
4-2 is later than reasonable for public safety under the conditions existing in
the town and advances the curfew time within its jurisdiction by one hour
during the Halloween period designated by the Council each year.
(Prior Code, § 130.01)
   (B)   (1)   No parent, guardian, or other person having the legal custody
and control of any minor person shall permit the minor to be or remain in or
upon any of the streets, alleys, parks, or other public places within the town
during curfew hours.
      (2)   Division (B)(1) above shall not apply if any minor is accompanied
by his or her parents or guardian or by any other person having the legal
custody and control of the minor; or if he or she is engaged in the performance
of a lawful errand, employment, or duly authorized and directed by the parent,
guardian, or other person having the legal care and custody of the minor, and
if the minor has in his or her possession a written permit from the person or
employer showing the reason and necessity to be upon or in the street, alley,
park, or other public place during curfew hours.
(Prior Code, § 130.02)
(Ord. 4, passed 4-8-1886; Ord. 316, passed 10-11-1976) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Curfew at local option, see I.C. 31-6-4-2
§ 130.02 FIREARMS.
   No person shall discharge a firearm, pellet gun, or B.B. gun within or into
the town except:
   (A)   By any authorized law enforcement officer in pursuit of his or her
duty;
   (B)   By any person who has reason to believe that he or she shall suffer an
imminent injury to his or her person or property and as a consequence
discharges the firearm, pellet gun, or B.B. gun in self-defense; or
   (C)   By any person exterminating rodents and reptiles generally considered
dangerous or offensive to public health and safety.
(Prior Code, § 130.20) Penalty, see §
130.99
Statutory reference:
   Firearms, see I.C. 35-47
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any parent, guardian, or other person having the legal care and
custody of the minor or minors who violate any provision of §
130.01 shall be fined not more than $2,500 for each offense.
   (C)   Any person violating the provisions of §
130.02 shall be fined not more than $2,500.
(Prior Code, § 130.99) (Ord. 4, passed 4-8-1886; Ord. 316, passed 10-11-1976)
TITLE XV: LAND USAGE
   Chapter
      
150.   PLANNING AND ZONING
CHAPTER 150: PLANNING AND ZONING
Section
   
150.01   House numbers; posting
   
150.02   Unified Development Code
 
   
150.99   Penalty
§ 150.01 HOUSE NUMBERS; POSTING.
   (A)   Property addressing. All residences and commercial, industrial, and
public structures located in the town shall be assigned a permanent city-type
street address. The assigned numbers shall be placed on the front of the
structure. In all situations, the Council shall make the final determination of
which street/road will be used for an address. The criteria for this
determination will be based on the layout of the property, layout of the
structure, and other factors that may affect emergency services and mail
delivery.
   (B)   Place and size of address numbers. It shall be the responsibility of
the property owner to provide visible and current address numbers on the
dwelling. The house numbers shall be at least three inches in height. The color
of the numbering shall be in contrast to its background. The letters or numbers
shall be placed on the main level in a location which faces, and is visible
from, the street upon which it is addressed, near a main entryway of the
dwelling, and placed higher than the height of the entryway.
   (C)   Enforcement. All properties within the jurisdiction of the town shall
be required to be in full compliance of this section within 90 days of the
passage date of the ordinance codified herein. It is the duty of the owner or
occupant of each house, business structure, or other structure within the town,
now existing or hereafter erected, which has been assigned a mailing address
for the receipt of mail through the United States Postal Service, to place on
such structure letters or numerals (not Roman numerals or written words) not
less than three inches high showing the number of the mailing address of each
such structure in the manner and location described in division (B) above. This
section is not intended to conflict with the United States Postal Service
requirements for mail delivery.
(Ord. 103-15, passed 4-13-2015) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.02 UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE.
   The Unified Development Code is adopted and incorporated as a part of this
code of ordinances as fully as if set out at length herein.
(Res. 20-104, passed 7-6-2020; Ord. 107-20, passed 8-10-2020; Ord. 108-20,
passed 8-10-2020; Ord. 100-21, passed 2-8-2021; Ord. 102-23, passed 5-8-2023;
Res. 23-101, passed 5-8-2023; Res. 23-102, passed 5-8-2023; Ord. 101-23, passed
6-12-2023; Ord. 106-25, passed 10-13-2025)
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   In the event that the owner or occupant of any such structure as
described in §
150.01(A) fails to comply with the terms and conditions of §
150.01, said owner or occupant, upon conviction, shall be guilty of an
infraction and subject to a fine of not less than $25 and not more than $50.
(Ord. 103-15, passed 4-13-2015)
